Lemma 11
.

Consider a column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, a canonical path
P
for column
c
and its last glue
\ell
on column
c
. Then,

e_{P_{0,\ldots,\ell}}\leq\mathcal{B}_{3}(c).

Proof.

Consider the useful prefix
Q
of
P
and the decomposition
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
into pseudo-visible glue on glue column
c
. We assume that
t\geq 2
: the case
t=0
is trivial. The case
t=1
can be dealt with Lemma
8
and the calculus are identical the ones done in this proof. Then by Lemma
9
, we can assume that there exist
u_{k^{\prime}}\leq k<{u_{k^{\prime}+1}}
such that
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{k}Q_{k+1})
is visible from the south on glue column
e_{Q}-1.5
. Let
0\leq s\leq|Q|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{s}Q_{s+1})
is visible from the south on glue column
c
in
Q
, we have either
s=u_{0}
or
s=u_{1}
. If
s=u_{0}
, let
n=u_{1}
otherwise let
n=u_{2}.
By the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
8
, let
u_{j^{\prime}}\leq j<u_{j^{\prime}+1}
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
has the same type as
\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})
and is the westernmost glue visible from the south in
Q
on glue column
c_{1}
with
c\leq c_{1}\leq e_{Q}-1.5
. Then by Lemma
10
we have

\displaystyle w_{Q_{j+1,\ldots,u_{j^{\prime}+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq w_{Q_{k+1,\ldots,u_{k^{\prime}+1}}}+(c_{1}-(e_{Q}-1.5))

By iterating this reasoning at most
|T|
times, we obtain:

\displaystyle w_{Q_{s,\ldots,n}}

\displaystyle\leq w_{Q_{k+1,\ldots,u_{k^{\prime}+1}}}+(c-(e_{Q}-1.5)+|T|

Since
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{u_{k^{\prime}+1}}Q_{u_{k^{\prime}+1}+1})
is on glue column
c
then
Q_{u_{k^{\prime}+1}}
is on column
c-0.5
. Thus, we have
w_{Q_{k+1,\ldots,u_{k^{\prime}+1}}}\leq c-0.5
:

\displaystyle w_{Q_{s,\ldots,n}}

\displaystyle\leq 2c-e_{Q}+|T|+1

By Lemma
7
, we have
w_{P}\geq w_{\sigma}-2|T|-1
which leads to:

w_{\sigma}-2|T|-1\leq 2c-e_{Q}+|T|+1

And then,

\displaystyle e_{Q}

\displaystyle\leq 2c-w_{\sigma}+3|T|+2

∎

5.4
Conclusion

Now, we can combine Corollary
1
and Lemma
11
together to obtain the main result of this section, Lemma
12
, which solves Lock
7
and prove the efficiency of the shield.

Note that this result is stronger than the one described in the roadmap (Section
3
). Indeed, Figure
12
shows a path
Q
growing on a tile of
P_{0,\ldots,\ell}
and turning around the shield. The following lemma claims that if the path
Q
grows far away from
P_{0,\ldots,\ell}
then it cannot come near it anymore. Thus, we are able to give this result even if a shield is not formally defined yet.

Lemma 12
.

Consider a path
P
which is canonical for glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
. Consider the index
\ell
of the last glue of
P
on column
c
. Consider a path
Q=Q^{\prime}Q^{\prime\prime}
such that:

•

Q_{0}
binds with a tile
P_{i}
of
P_{0,\ldots,\ell}
;

•

the last tile of
Q^{\prime}
is the easternmost one and
e_{Q^{\prime}}\geq\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
;

•

P_{0,\ldots,i}Q^{\prime}
is a producible path.

Then
P_{0,\ldots,i}Q
is a producible path.

Proof.

By Lemma
11
, we have
e_{P_{0,\ldots,\ell}}\leq\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)
. Then
w_{Q^{\prime}}\leq\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1
and thus
Q^{\prime}
crosses the glue column
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1.5
, see Figure
30
. Remark that since
e_{P_{0,\ldots,\ell}}\leq\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)
, the glues of
Q^{\prime}
which are visible on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1.5
are also visible in
R=P_{0,\ldots,i}Q^{\prime}
. Thus, we can define
(s,n)
the span of
R
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1.5
. Without loss of generality, we suppose that this span is an upward span and let
\mathcal{C}
be the workspace of the span
(s,n)
of
R
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1.5
. Since
e_{P_{0,\ldots,\ell}}\leq\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)
and the last tile of
Q
is the easternmost one then
\mathrm{glue}(R_{s+1}R_{s+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(R_{n+1}R_{n+1})
both point east and no tile of
P_{0,\ldots,\ell}
is inside
\mathcal{C}
. Now if
Q^{\prime\prime}
is inside
\mathcal{C}
then
P_{0,\ldots,i}Q
is producible and the lemma is true.

Otherwise let
S
be the shortest prefix of
Q^{\prime\prime}
which is not entirely inside
\mathcal{C}
. Since
Q^{\prime}Q^{\prime\prime}
is a path, then
S
must cross
l^{n}
or
l^{s}
. Both cases are symmetric thus assume that
S
crosses
l^{n}
. Then
x_{S_{|S|-1}}=\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1
and since
e_{P_{0,\ldots,\ell}}\leq\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)
then
S
does not intersect with
P_{0,\ldots,\ell}
and
RS
is a producible path. By hypothesis, we have
e_{RS}\geq\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Moreover, the tile
S_{|S|-1}
is visible from the south on column
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1
and
\mathrm{glue}(R_{s}R_{s+1})
is visible from the south in
RS
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1.5
. All these hypotheses contradict Corollary
1
.

s
l^{s}
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
\mathcal{B}_{3}(c)+1.5
n

. Consider a path

6
Decomposition into dominant arcs

Consider an extremal path
P
which is canonical for a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
. A key point of the roadmap was to use a shield in order to protect a glue of
P
on glue column
c
, see Subsection
3.6
. In the previous example of Figure
8
, we assumed that this protected glue was pseudo-visible, see Lock
5
. Unfortunately, this is not always the case, see Figure
31
. We need to focus on the other glues of
P
on glue column
c
. To do so, we consider subpaths of
P
whose first and last glues are two consecutive glues on glue column
c
. These subpaths will be called
arcs
of
P
.

The notion of arcs is properly defined in Subsection
6.1
alongside the more general concept of
\emph{holes}
. Then, we focus on the subpaths of a path which are arcs, see Subsection
6.2
, and we analyze their properties, see Subsection
6.3
. These results lead to the definition of the decomposition of an extremal path into dominant arcs in Subsection
6.4
. This decomposition will be the one used in the final Section
7
.
Indeed, the previous decomposition into pseudo-visible glues has been useful to define canonical paths, see Section
4
. Nevertheless, canonical paths have only three properties which will be relevant in the final Section
7
: the first one is Lemma
11
(the bound on
P_{0,\ldots,\ell}
), the second one is Lemma
12
(the final result of Section
5
) and the third one is achieved here in Corollary
3
. This result is obtained by studying how the priority of the pseudo-spans affects the decomposition of a canonical path into dominant arcs.

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{m}

Figure 31:
A producible path
P
with its span
(s,n)
on a glue column
c
. The blue dots represents a shield growing on the span
(s,n)
which protects a glue
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m}P_{m+1})
. The suffix
P_{m,\ldots,|P|-1}
of
P
is red to show that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m}P_{m+1})
is not pseudo-visible from the south in
P
.

6.1
Holes and arcs

We start with some basic definitions. Consider a path
H
which is a subassembly of the terminal assembly
\gamma
8
8

8

This hypothesis is important and we will always consider paths that can be assembled by the tile assembly system.
. Suppose that
\mathrm{glue}(H_{0}H_{1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(H_{|H|-2}H_{|H|-1})
are both on the same glue column
c
, we define

as the finite line between
\mathrm{glue}(H_{0}H_{1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(H_{|H|-2}H_{|H|-1})
. We say that

on glue column

if and only if the only intersection between

and

are
\mathrm{glue}(H_{0}H_{1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(H_{|H|-2}H_{|H|-1})
, see Figure

. The finite line
l^{H}
is called the

of the hole. We say that the hole
H
is an
upward
(resp.

) hole if and only if
y_{H_{0}}<y_{H_{|H|-1}}
(resp.
y_{H_{0}}>y_{H_{|H|-1}}
).

. Thus, we also have:

Fact 5
.

For a hole
H
, we have
w_{H}\leq w_{\min(\mathcal{I}_{H})}<e_{\min(\mathcal{I}_{H})}\leq e_{H}
.

If
\mathrm{glue}(H_{0}H_{1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(H_{|H|-2}H_{|H|-1})
are the only two glues of
H
which are on glue column
c
then
H
is called an
arc
. In this case, if
x_{H_{1,\ldots,|H|-2}}>c
, we say that
H
is a
positive
arc and
\mathrm{glue}(H_{0}H_{1})
points east while
\mathrm{glue}(H_{|H|-2}H_{|H|-1})
points west. Otherwise, we have
x_{H_{1,\ldots,|H|-2}}<c
and
H
is a
negative
arc and
\mathrm{glue}(H_{0}H_{1})
points west while
\mathrm{glue}(H_{|H|-2}H_{|H|-1})
points east. Finally, here is a technical lemma which will be useful later.

Lemma 13
.

Consider a hole
H
on glue column
c
and its interior
\mathcal{I}_{H}
. If
A
is a positive arc of glue column
c
which is inside
\mathcal{I}_{H}
(apart from its first and last tiles) then there exist
0\leq i\leq j\leq|H|-1
such that
H_{i,\ldots,j}
is a positive arc on glue column
c
with

\min\{y_{H_{i}},y_{H_{j}}\}\leq\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}\leq\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}\leq\max\{y_{H_{i}},y_{H_{j}}\}.

Proof.

Consider
0\leq k\leq|A|-1
such that
x_{A_{k}}=e_{A}
and consider the ray
l^{k}
starting at position
\mathrm{pos}(A_{k})
and going east, see Figure
34
. If this ray encounter no tile of
H
then
A_{k}
(and thus
A
) cannot be inside
\mathcal{I}_{H}
since this area is finite. Now suppose that there exists
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq|H|-1
such that
H_{k^{\prime}}
is on
l^{k}
. Since
H
is hole there exists
i\leq k^{\prime}\leq j
such that
H_{i,\ldots,j}
is a positive arc of glue column
c
. If we have
\min\{y_{H_{i}},y_{H_{j}}\}\leq\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}\leq\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}\leq\max\{y_{H_{i}},y_{H_{j}}\}
then the lemma is true
9
9

9

Note that we are looking for a necessary condition not a sufficient one.
. Otherwise, if
\max\{y_{H_{i}},y_{H_{j}}\}\leq\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}
, it is possible to modify the ray
l^{k}
such that it turns around this obstacle by going north without crossing glue column
c
, see Figure
34
. A similar reasoning can be done if
\min\{y_{H_{i}},y_{H_{j}}\}\geq\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}
. Finally, if
\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}<y_{H_{i}}<\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}
then there are two possibilities. The first one is that
H_{i,\ldots,j}
crosses through
A
. In this case at least one glue of
A
is not inside
\mathcal{I}_{H}
and
A
cannot be inside
\mathcal{I}_{H}
. The other case is that
H_{k}
is a tile of
A
and the start-point of
l^{k}
, see Figure
34
, and we also have
\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}<y_{H_{j}}<\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}
. Nevertheless, the arc
H_{i,\ldots,j}
is not sufficient to show that
A
is inside
\mathcal{I}_{H}
.

6.2
Arcs of a path

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile if the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}

and two indices
0\leq i<j\leq|P|-1
. We say that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an
arc
of
P
on glue column
c
if and only if
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is a positive arc on glue column
c
. Indeed, with the chosen conventions, we focus mainly on the east side of glue column
c
and thus on the positive arc. Note that since
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is positive then
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i}P_{i+1})
must point east and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
must point west.

Now we introduce some notions to compare and to order the different arcs of
P
, see Figure
35
for illustrations.
If
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an upward (resp. downward) arc, the
next
glue of
P
according to arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is defined as the southernmost (resp. northernmost) glue of
P
among the glues which are on glue column
c
and strictly north (resp. south) of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j-1}P_{j})
10
10

10

Note that the next glue is always correctly defined since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j-1}P_{j})
points west and thus cannot be one of the two glues of
P
which are visible from the north or south since they both point east by Lemma
1
and Lemma
6
.
,
i.e.
the next glue is on glue column
c
and its
y
-coordinate is:

P_{0}
P_{\ell}
Q_{0}
Q_{\ell}
R_{0}
R_{\ell}

\mathrm{glue}(P_{\ell}P_{\ell+1})
.
In the second example, the path

has two dominant arcs, the upward arc in yellow is south and anterior of the upward arc in orange. The next glue according to the yellow arc is the first glue of the orange arc and the next glue according the orange arc is
Q_{\ell}
. In the third example, the path
R
has two dominant arcs, the upward arc in orange is south and anterior of the downward arc in blue. The next glue of both these arcs is
\mathrm{glue}(R_{\ell}R_{\ell+1})
.

\displaystyle\min\{y:\text{there }

exists

\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})
is

\displaystyle\text{on glue column $c$, $y_{P_{k}}=y$ and $y>y_{P_{j}}$ (resp. $y<y_{P_{j}}$)}\}.

Now, consider another arc
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
on glue column
c
. We say that
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is
north
of
P_{i,\ldots,j}
(and
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is
south
of
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
) if and only if
\max\{y_{P_{i}},y_{P_{j}}\}<\min\{y_{P_{i^{\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}\}.
Now, if

\min\{y_{P_{i}},y_{P_{j}}\}<\min\{y_{P_{i^{\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}\}<\max\{y_{P_{i^{\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}\}<\max\{y_{P_{i}},y_{P_{j}}\},

we say that the arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}

dominates

P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
. If an arc
P
is not dominated by another arc of
P
, we say that this arc is
dominant
. Note that the case

\min\{y_{P_{i}},y_{P_{j}}\}<\min\{y_{P_{i^{\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}\}<\max\{y_{P_{i}},y_{P_{j}}\}<\max\{y_{P_{i^{\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}\}

is not possible otherwise the arcs
P_{i,\ldots,j}
and
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
would intersect, contradicting the fact that
P
is a path (which is self-avoiding by definition). Thus, when considering two dominant arcs, one has to be north of the other one. Finally, if
j<i^{\prime}
, we say that the arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is
anterior
to the arc
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
otherwise
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is
posterior
to the arc
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
.

To summarize, the notion of anteriority/posteriority induces a total order on the arcs of
P
(according to their appearances in
P
). The relation of domination induces a partial order on the arcs of
P
and the notion of south/north induces a total order on the dominant arcs of
P
. The main result of the following section is to show that the order induced by the anteriority/posteriority restricted to the upward dominant arcs (resp. downward dominant arcs) is equivalent to the order induced by the notion of south/north.

6.3
Properties of dominant arcs

The next subsection focus on analyzing the relationship between the three orders introduced in Subsection
6.2
. To do so, we consider an arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
of
P
and we focus on its next
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
, the first step is to show that
i<j<i^{\prime}
.

Lemma 14
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, two indices
0<i<j<|P|-1
such that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and the index
i^{\prime}
of the next glue of
P
according to this arc. Then, we have
i<j<i^{\prime}
.

Proof.

For the sake of contradiction suppose that
i^{\prime}\leq j
. Then
i^{\prime}<i
since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
is neither
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i}P_{i+1})
nor
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
and no other glue of the arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is on glue column
c
. Consider the path
H=P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j}
. By definition of
i^{\prime}
as the index of the next glue of
P
according to arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
then
H
is a hole, see Figure
36
. Let
l^{H}
be the frontier of this hole and
\mathcal{I}_{H}
be its interior. We remind that
\mathcal{I}_{H}
is the finite area delimited by the cut of the
2D
plane done by
l^{H}
and
H
. We consider that the curve generated by
l^{H}
and
H
has the same orientation as
H
. Since
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is dominant then if
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an upward (resp. downward) arc then
\mathcal{I}_{H}
is the left (resp. right) side of the cut done by
l^{H}
and
P_{i,\ldots,j}
. By definition of an arc of
P
,
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is a positive arc for glue column
c
. Thus,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j-1}P_{j})
points west implying that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
turns left (resp. right) of the curve generated by
P_{i,\ldots,j}
and
l^{H}
at the position of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j-1}P_{j})
. Thus, the tile
P_{j}
is inside
\mathcal{I}_{H}
. By hypothesis, the last tile of
P
is the easternmost one implying that
P_{|P|-1}
cannot be inside the finite area
\mathcal{I}_{H}
then
P_{j,\ldots,|P|-1}
must intersect with either
P_{k,\ldots,j-1}
contradicting the fact that
P
is simple or it must cross
l^{H}
contradicting the definition of
i^{\prime}
. Then
j<i^{\prime}
.

P_{i}
P_{j}
\mathcal{I}_{H}
H_{0}=P_{i^{\prime}}

Lemma 15
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
0\leq\ell<|P|-1
be the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. Consider two indices
0<i<j<|P|-1
such that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc on glue column
c
and the index of the next glue
i^{\prime}
of
P
according to this arc. Then, if
i^{\prime}<\ell
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
points east then there exists
i^{\prime}<j^{\prime}<\ell
such that
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is a dominant arc of
P
which is north (resp. south) and posterior of
P_{i,\ldots,j}
.

Proof.

We consider that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an upward arc, the other case is symmetric, see Figure
37
. Consider
j^{\prime}=\min\{k>i^{\prime}+1:\mathrm{glue}(P_{k-1}P_{k})\text{ is on glue column }c\}
. By definition of
j^{\prime}
,
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is an arc of glue column
c
. By hypothesis,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
points east implying that
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is a positive arc and thus an arc of
P
. By Lemma
14
,
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is posterior of
P_{i,\ldots,j}
.

P_{i}
P_{j}
P_{i^{\prime}}
P_{j^{\prime}}

?

Proof of Lemma
15
. Consider an upward dominant arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
of a path
P
on glue column
c
and the next
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
of
P
according to this arc. If
i^{\prime}
is not the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column

then there exists
j^{\prime}>i^{\prime}
such that
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is an arc. This arc is posterior to
P_{i,\ldots,j}
since

by Lemma

. Also,

must be an upward arc otherwise it would dominate
P_{i,\ldots,j}
. Now if an arc
A
of

dominates
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
then one of its extremities is on the red part of glue column

. The other extremity must either be on the green line (which correspond to the line
b
in the proof) contradicting the definition of

or the yellow line (contradicting that

is simple) or the blue ray (contradicting that

is dominant).

\mathrm{glue}(P_{j-1}P_{j})
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
. By definition of
i^{\prime}
the only intersections between
P
and

\mathrm{glue}(P_{j-1}P_{j})
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
. Since
P_{i,\ldots,j}

y_{P_{i^{\prime}}}>y_{P_{j}}>y_{P_{i}}
.
Suppose that an arc
A
of
P
dominates
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
then we have
\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}<y_{P_{i^{\prime}}}<\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}
. Since
A
cannot intersect
b
and

then we have
\max\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}>y_{P_{i^{\prime}}}>y_{P_{j}}>y_{P_{i}}>\min\{y_{A_{0}},y_{A_{|A|-1}}\}
. In this case,
A
would also dominates the arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
of
P
which is a contradiction. Then
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
.
Finally, if
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is a downward arc then since
P_{i^{\prime}+1,\ldots,j^{\prime}}
cannot intersect with
b
and
P_{i,\ldots,j}
and we would have
y_{P_{i^{\prime}}}>y_{P_{j}}>y_{P_{i}}>y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}
. In other words,
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
would dominates
P_{i,\ldots,j}
which is a contradiction. Then,
P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is an upward arc of
P
and we have
y_{P_{j^{\prime}}}>y_{P_{i^{\prime}}}>y_{P_{j}}>y_{P_{i}}
,
i.e.

P_{i^{\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime}}
is north of
P_{i,\ldots,j}
.
∎

Finally the last case is when
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
points west. This case leads to a contradiction and is impossible.

Lemma 16
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, two indices
0<i<j<|P|-1
such that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and the index
i^{\prime}
of the next glue of
P
according to this arc. Then,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
points east.

Proof.

We consider that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an upward arc, the other case is symmetric, see Figure
38
. For the sake of contradiction suppose that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i^{\prime}}P_{i^{\prime}+1})
points west and consider
j^{\prime}=\max\{k<i^{\prime}:\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})\text{ is on glue column }c\}
. By definition of
j^{\prime}
,
P_{j^{\prime},\ldots,i^{\prime}+1}
is an arc of
P
on glue column
c
. By a reasoning similar to the one of the proof of Lemma
15
,
P_{j^{\prime},\ldots,i^{\prime}+1}
is a dominant downward arc. By Lemma
14
, we have
i<j<i^{\prime}
and thus
i<j<j^{\prime}<i^{\prime}
since the two arcs share no tile in common. Consider the path
H=P_{j-1,\ldots,i^{\prime}+1}
. By definition of
i^{\prime}
as the index of the next glue of
P
according to arc
P_{i,\ldots,j}
then
H
is a hole. By a reasoning similar to the one of the proof of Lemma
14
, the tile
P_{j}
is inside the interior
\mathcal{I}_{H}
of the hole
H
. This is a contradiction since the last tile of
P
is the easternmost one.

must be upward (resp. downward) arcs. Moreover, for upward (resp. downward) arcs the orders implied by the notions of south/north and anteriority/posteriority are equivalent. Finally, the last remaining lemma of this subsection shows that the smallest element of this order is the arc starting by
P_{s}
(resp.

).

Lemma 17
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
0\leq\ell<|P|-1
be the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq s\leq|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is visible from the south (resp. north) in
P
on glue column
c
. Then, we have either
s=\ell
or there exists
s<j<\ell
such that
P_{s,\ldots,j}
is an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc of
P
such that no arc of
P
is south (resp. north) of
P_{s,\ldots,j}
.

Proof.

Up to symmetries, we can consider that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is visible from the south and let
l^{s}
be its glue ray. If
s=\ell
then the lemma is true, otherwise we can define
j=\min\{k>s:\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})\text{ is on glue column }c\}
. Since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
cannot be on
l^{s}
then
y_{P_{j}}>y_{P_{s}}
and
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is an upward arc. Moreover, by Lemma
6
and Lemma
1
,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is pointing east then
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is a positive arc and then an arc of
P
. Since the only intersection between
P
and
l^{s}
is
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
, then no arc of
P
can dominate
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
and thus
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is a dominant arc of
P
. Moreover, no other arc of
P
can intersect
l^{s}
and be south of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
.
∎

6.4
Decomposition into dominant arcs

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
0\leq\ell<|P|-1
be the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. Consider
0<s<|P|-1
and
0<n<|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is visible from the south on glue column
c
in
P
and
0<n<|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
is visible from the north on glue column
c
in
P
.

We introduce the
upward (resp. downward) decomposition of

P

into dominant arcs
on glue column
c
which is made of two sequences of indices
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
such that
m_{0}=s
(resp.
m_{0}=n
),
m_{t}=\ell
and for all
0\leq i<t
,
P_{m_{i},\ldots,b_{i}}
is an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc of
P
and if
i<t
,
m_{i+1}
is the index of the next glue of
P
according to
P_{m_{i},\ldots,b_{i}}
. Lemmas
15
,
16
and
17
imply that this decomposition is correctly defined and that if
i<t-1
then the arc
P_{m_{i+1},\ldots,b_{i+1}}
is north (resp. south) and posterior of arc
P_{m_{i},\ldots,b_{i}}
.

Fact 6
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one and a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
. Consider
0\leq j<k\leq|P|-1
such that
P_{j,\ldots,k}
is an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc of
P
then consider
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs. Then, there exists
0\leq a<t
such
P_{j,\ldots,k}=P_{m_{a},\ldots,b_{a}}
.

The indices
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
are called the indices of the
southern main
(resp.
northern main
) glues of
P
on glue column
c
and these glues all point east while the glues whose indices are
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
all point west and are called the
southern backup
(resp.
northern backup
) glues of
P
. They will allow a better characterization of the workspace of the span of
P
on glue column
c
, see Figures
40
,
41
and
42
for an illustration of the following notions. We define
b^{0}
as the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
(resp.
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
) to the south (resp. north). We define
b^{t+1}
as the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{|P|-2}P_{|P|-1})
going south (resp. north). For each
0<i\leq t
, we define
b^{i}
as the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{i-1}-1}P_{b_{i-1}})
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{i}}P_{m_{i}+1})
. These segments are called the
frontiers
of the decomposition. Remark that, by the definition of the next glue, for all
0<i\leq t
,
P
intersects
b^{i}
only at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{i-1}-1}P_{b_{i-1}})
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{i}}P_{m_{i}+1})
. Thus,
P_{b_{i-1}-1,\ldots,m_{i}+1}
is a hole of
P
on glue column
c
whose interior is delimited by
P_{b_{i-1}-1,\ldots,m_{i}+1}
and its frontier
b^{i}
. To simplify the notations, we designate by
\mathcal{I}_{i}
the area
\mathcal{I}_{P_{b_{i-1}-1,\ldots,m_{i}+1}}
and call
(\mathcal{I}_{i})_{0<i\leq t}
the
interiors
of the decomposition.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be the workspace of the span of
P
on glue column
c
(this span is
(s,n)
if
s\leq n
and
(n,s)
otherwise). This workspace can be cut in three parts: the area
\mathcal{C}_{3}
which the intersection of
\mathcal{C}
with the half-plane defined by
x>e_{\gamma}
(no tile of
\gamma
is inside this area); the area
\mathcal{C}_{2}
is the left side of the area delimited by
l^{n}
,
P_{n,\ldots,|P|-1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{pos}(P_{|P|-1})
and going north; the area
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is the right side of the area delimited by
l^{s}
,
P_{s,\ldots,|P|-1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{pos}(P_{|P|-1})
and going south. Note that, for all
0<i\leq t
,
\mathcal{I}_{i}
is included into
\mathcal{C}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2}
). Now, we definite
\mathcal{E}
as the area delimited by:

b^{0},P_{s,\ldots,b_{0}},b^{1},P_{m_{1},\ldots,b_{1}},b^{2}\ldots b^{t},P_{\ell,\ldots,|P|-1},b^{t+1}.

This area is also included into
\mathcal{C}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2}
) since it is the intersection of this area with the half-plane delimited by
x\geq c
. Then
\mathcal{C}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2}
) is the union of
\mathcal{E}
and all the interiors
\mathcal{I}_{i}
for all
0<i\leq t
. Moreover,
\mathcal{E}
is connected to all the interiors whereas an interior
\mathcal{I}_{i}
is connected only to
\mathcal{E}
via
b^{i}
. Indeed, consider
0<i<j\leq t
and a path
Q
such that
Q_{0}
is in
\mathcal{I}_{i}
and
Q_{|Q|-1}
is in
\mathcal{I}_{j}
then a prefix a
Q
intersects with
b^{i}
to exit
\mathcal{I}_{i}
and enter inside
\mathcal{E}
whereas a suffix of
Q
intersects with
b^{j}
to exit
\mathcal{E}
and enter inside
\mathcal{I}_{j}
.

P_{s}
P_{n}
\mathcal{C}_{3}
\mathcal{C}_{2}
\mathcal{C}_{1}

6.5
Links between the two decompositions

Indices of the decomposition of
P
into dominant arcs are not linked with the indices
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t^{\prime}}
of the decomposition of
P
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. Indeed, a northern or southern main glues may not be pseudo-visible (see Figure
31
) and a pseudo-visible
\mathrm{glue}(P_{u_{i}}P_{u_{i}+1})
is not necessary a main glue of
P
, see Figure
43
. Nevertheless, some correspondencies can still be done. First, we introduce the notion of weakly dominance which is a restriction of the notion of dominance to a pseudo-span of
P
, see Figure
43
.

P_{s}=P_{m_{0}}=P_{u_{0}}
P_{n}=P_{m_{0}^{\prime}}=P_{u_{1}}
P_{u_{2}}
P_{u_{3}}
P_{u_{4}}
P_{u_{5}}=\ell

Figure 43:
The decomposition of the path
P
from Figure
31
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. The index of the last glue of
P
is
\ell
. The upward pseudo-spans
(u_{0},u_{1})

(u_{4},u_{5})
are in yellow and red. The upward pseudo-span
(u_{2},u_{3})
is in orange and red. The red tiles correspond to the dominant arcs of the upward pseudo-spans and they are all upward arcs. The downward pseudo-span
(u_{1},u_{2})
is in light blue and dark blue. The downward pseudo-span
(u_{3},u_{4})
is in blue and dark blue. The dark blue tiles correspond to the dominant arcs of the downward pseudo-spans and they are all downward arcs.

Definition 6
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and the decomposition
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
of
P
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq i<t
and
u_{i}\leq j<k<u_{i+1}
such that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an arc of
P
, we say that this arc is
weakly dominant
if and only there no
u_{i}\leq a<b<u_{i+1}
such that
P_{a,\ldots,b}
is an arc of
P
which dominates
P_{j,\ldots,k}
.

Then, we show that a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
is weakly dominant (Lemma
18
and Corollary
2
) for some pseudo-span of
P
on glue column
c
. Afterwards, we show that if a pseudo-span
(u_{i},u_{i+1})
is an upward (resp. downward) pseudo-span then any weakly dominant arc of this pseudo-span is also an upward (resp. downward) arc (see Lemma
19
). These two lemmas imply the main result of this section in Corollary
3
: the orientation of the spans matches the orientation of the dominant arcs, thus assembling a path which turns right of such an arc and which intersects with the end of
P
would contradict the definition of a canonical path.

Lemma 18
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and the decomposition
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
of
P
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq j<k<|P|-1
such that
P_{j,\ldots,k}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
. Then, there exists
0\leq i<t
such that
u_{i}\leq j<k<u_{i+1}
.

Proof.

Up to symmetries, we can consider that
P_{j,\ldots,k}
is an upward dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
. We remind that
u_{t}
is, by definition, the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
and since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})
is on glue column
c
then
j<k\leq u_{t}
. By Fact
6
,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
is also a main southern glue.
Let
0\leq s\leq|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is visible from the south in
P
on glue column
c
. Remind that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is either
u_{0}
or
u_{1}
depending if the span of
P
is upward or downward and that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is also the first main southern glue of
P
. Then, we have
u_{0}\leq s\leq j
. Thus, there exists
0\leq i<t
such that
u_{i}\leq j<u_{i+1}
. Note that, since
k=\min\{a>j:\mathrm{glue}(P_{a}P_{a+1})\text{ is on glue column $c$}\}
and since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{u_{i+1}}P_{u_{i+1}+1})
is also on glue column
c
then
u_{i}\leq j<k\leq u_{i+1}
. Moreover, since
P_{j\ldots,k}
is a positive arc then
\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})
points west while
\mathrm{glue}(P_{u_{i+1}}P_{u_{i+1}+1})
points east (as all glues of the decomposition of
P
into pseudo-visible glues) then
k<u_{i+1}
.
∎

Corollary 2
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and the decomposition
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
of
P
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq j<k<|P|-1
such that
P_{j,\ldots,k}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and
0\leq i\leq t
such that
u_{i}\leq j<k<u_{i+1}
. Then,
P_{j,\ldots,k}
is weakly dominant for the pseudo-span
(u_{i},u_{i+1})
of
P
.

Lemma 19
.

Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and the decomposition
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
of
P
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq j<k<|P|-1
such that
P_{j,\ldots,k}
is an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and
0\leq i\leq t
such that
u_{i}\leq j<k<u_{i+1}
. Then,
(u_{i},u_{i+1})
is an upward (resp. downward) pseudo-span of
P
.

Proof.

In fact, this proof follows the steps and reasonings which were done in Subsection
6.3
, see Figure
44
.
Consider a producible path
P
whose last tile is the easternmost one, a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and the decomposition
(u_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
of
P
into pseudo-visible glues on glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq i<t
such that
(u_{i},u_{i+1})
is a pseudo-span of
P
on glue column
c
. Without loss of generality, we suppose that
(u_{i},u_{i+1})
is an upward pseudo-span. Let
s=u_{i}
and
n=u_{i+1}
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is pseudo-visible from the south in
P
while
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
is visible from the north in
P_{s,\ldots,|P|-1}
. Let
j=\min\{k>s:\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})\text{ is on glue column }c\}
, thus
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is an arc of
P
(
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
points east since it is a glue of the decomposition). By definition of
j
, we have
j<n
since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
points east and is also on glue column
c
. Since
P_{s,\ldots,|P|-1}
cannot cross
l^{s}
(the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
going south) then
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is an upward arc and no arc of
P_{s,\ldots,|P|-1}
can dominate or be south of
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
. Then
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is the southernmost weakly dominant arc of the span
(s,n)
of
P
. Now let
a
be the index of the southernmost glue of
P_{s,\ldots,n+1}
which is strictly to the north of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
. If
a=n
then
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
is the only weakly dominant arc of the span
(s,n)
of
P
(since
P_{s,\ldots,n+1}
can intersect the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
going north only at its start-point). Thus the lemma is true in this case. Then suppose that
a<n
.

P_{s}=P_{u_{0}}
P_{n}=P_{u_{1}}

Figure 44:
The span
(s,n)
of the path
P
from Figure
31
. There are three arcs in
P_{0,\ldots,n}
. The red one is dominant (and thus weakly dominant). The orange one is weakly dominant but not dominant (another arc of
P_{n,\ldots,|P|-1}
dominates this arc, see Figure
39
). The last one in yellow is dominated by the red arc. All weakly dominant arcs are upwards arcs since
(s,n)
is an upward span.

Firstly, for the sake of contradiction, suppose that
a<s<j

11
11

11

One could argue that
a
belongs to
P_{s,\ldots,n}
but we will not used this argument here since we present a sketch of the proof of Lemma
14
which can be used in the general case.
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
14
,
P_{a,\ldots,j+1}
is a hole and
P_{j+1}
is in the interior of this hole (since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
points west). Since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
is the northernmost glue of
P_{s,\ldots,n}
, it cannot be inside the interior of this hole. Thus
P_{j+1,\ldots,n}
must intersect with either
P_{a,\ldots,j}
(which is not possible since
P
is simple) or the frontier of the hole (contradicting the definition of
a
). Thus, we have
s<j<a
.

Secondly for the sake of contradiction suppose that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{a}P_{a+1})
points west. Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
16
,
P_{j,\ldots,a}
is a hole and
P_{a+1}
is inside the interior of this hole (since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{a}P_{a+1})
points west). Since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
is the northernmost glue of
P_{s,\ldots,n}
, it cannot be inside the interior of this hole. Thus,
P_{a+1,\ldots,n}
must intersect with either
P_{j,\ldots,a}
(which is not possible since
P
is simple) or the frontier of the hole (contradicting the definition of
a
). Thus,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{a}P_{a+1})
points east.

Then let
b=\min\{k>a:\mathrm{glue}(P_{k}P_{k+1})\text{ is on glue column }c\}
and thus
P_{a,\ldots,b+1}
is an arc of
P
. By definition of
j
, we have
j<n
since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{n}P_{n+1})
points east and is also on glue column
c
. Also, since
P_{a+1,\ldots,b+1}
cannot cross
l^{s}
,
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
and the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{a}P_{a+1})
, then
P_{a,\ldots,b+1}
is an upward arc of
P
. Similarly, no arc of
P_{s,\ldots,n}
can dominate
P_{a,\ldots,b+1}
since it would need to cross either
l^{s}
,
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
or the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{j}P_{j+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(P_{a}P_{a+1})
. Then
P_{a,\ldots,b+1}
is a weakly dominant upward arc of the span
(s,n)
of
P
which is north of the arc
P_{s,\ldots,j+1}
of the same span. By iterating this reasoning, we obtain that any weakly dominant arc of the span
(s,n)
of
P
is an upward arc of
P
and the lemma is true.

∎

When the path
P
is a canonically path for glue column
c
, it means that an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
would be a subpath of an upward (resp. downward) pseudo-span of
P
. The definition of a canonical path leads to the following corollary.

Corollary 3
.

Consider a glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
P
be a canonical path of glue column
c
. Consider
0\leq i<j<|P|-1
such that
P_{i,\ldots,j}
is an upward (resp. downward) dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
then let
0\leq s\leq|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is visible from the south (resp. north) in
P
. Consider a path
B
such that
P_{0,\ldots,k}B
is a path for some
i<k<j
,
B_{0}\neq P_{k+1}
and
B
is inside the area delimited by
l^{s}
,
P_{s,\ldots,|P|-1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{pos}(P_{|P|-1})
and going south (resp. north). Then
B
does not intersect with
P
.

7
Shield and conclusion

This section aims at showing that shields can be found and that whenever two identical protected glues are found on two glue columns on different canonical paths, either shields can be used to translate paths to go beyond the eastern limit of the assembly or a new protected glue can be found. Eventually, we obtain a contradiction and this concludes the proof.

We start by defining a shield in Subsection
7.1
. Then, we present the three kinds of protected glues and their properties in Subsection
7.2
. Afterwards, we explain how to combine two protected main glues of the same type in Subsection
7.3
in order to find a new protected glue. Similarly, we explain how to patch the previous results to combine two protected backup glues of the same type in Subsection
7.4
and finally we conclude with the main result in Theorem
2
.

7.1
Shield

Before introducing the formal definition of a shield, we need to define the index of the
first glue
of
P
on glue column
c
as

\min\{i:\mathrm{glue}(P_{i}P_{i+1})\text{ is on glue column $c$}\}.

The definition of a shield is illustrated in Figure
45
.

Definition 7
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
. Let
0\leq f<|P|-1
be the index of the first glue of
P
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and
0\leq\ell<|P|-1
the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. Consider
0<s\leq|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{s}P_{s+1})
is visible from the south (resp. north) in
P_{0,\ldots,f+1}
on glue column
c
and let
\mathcal{C}
be the right (resp. left) side of the area delimited by
l^{s}
,
P_{s,\ldots,f+1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and going south (resp. north). A path
S
is a southern (resp. northern)
shield
of
P
on glue column
c
at position
s\leq a\leq f
iff:

•

S
is a sub-assembly of
\gamma

•

S
does not intersect with
P_{s,\ldots,f+1}
and
S_{1,\ldots,|S|-1}
is in
\mathcal{C}
;

•

A=SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
is an upward (resp. downward) negative arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
;

•

SP_{a}
is a prefix of
\min(\mathcal{I}_{A})
.

•

w_{A}\leq c
;

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{\ell}
S_{0}=A_{0}
P_{a}
P_{f}=A_{|A|-2}

Figure 45:
A path
P
which is canonical for a glue column
c
and its upward span
(s,n)
on glue column
c
.
\ell
is the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
and
f
is the index of the first glue of
P
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. The colored (blue and light blue) area
\mathcal{C}
represents the right side of the area delimited by
l^{s}
,
P_{s,\ldots,f}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and going south. The path
S
(the blue dots) is a southern shield of
P
on glue column
c
at position
a
: the path
A=SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
(the blue dots and tiles) is an upward negative arc of column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
which intersects with glue column
c
. Moreover, the interior
\mathcal{I}_{A}
of the arc
A
is in blue and is inside
\mathcal{C}
. Finally, note that
SP_{a}
is a prefix of
\min(\mathcal{I}_{A})
.

Here are some remarks about this definition. Firstly, we will mainly work on
P_{0,\ldots,f+1}
in this section. By lemma
11
, we have
s\leq\ell<f
implying that the indices of all glues of
P
on glue column
c
are smaller than
f
. For example, the glue visible from the south in
P
and
P_{0,\ldots,f+1}
is the same one. Thus, not taking the end of the path
P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}
into account is not problematic for our study. This subpath
P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}
is only relevant, in some cases, when it is copied and pasted to the east to obtain a contradiction. Secondly, we remind that
A
is self-avoiding by definition. Thus there is no intersection between
S
and
P_{a,\ldots,f+1}
(but intersections between
S
and
P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}
may exist). Thirdly, since
A
is a negative arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
then
\mathrm{glue}(S_{0}S_{1})
is on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and points west. Moreover, the tile
S_{|S|-1}
must bind with
P_{a}
. We also have
e_{A_{1\ldots,|A|-2}}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and more importantly, see Figure
45
:

Fact 7
.

Consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, with
0\leq s\leq|P|-1
as the index of a glue visible on glue column
c
in
P
and
f
as the index of its first glue on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Consider a shield
S
of
P
on glue column
c
at position
s\leq a\leq f
and let
A=SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
. Then, the interior
\mathcal{I}_{A}
of arc
A
is included into
\mathcal{C}
.

This fact is implicitly used every time we assemble a path: we always work into areas where there is no tile of the seed
\sigma
and the beginning of the path. Finally, the third condition of definition
7
implies that there is no “shortcut” in
\mathcal{I}_{A}
between a tile of
S
and another tile of
A
,
i.e.
consider
0\leq i\leq j\leq|A|-1
such that
i\leq|S|-1
then for any path
Q
which is sub-assembly of
\gamma
and such that
Q
is in
\mathcal{I}_{A}
,
Q_{0}=A_{i}
and
Q_{|Q|-1}=A_{j}
then
S_{i,\ldots,|S|-1}P_{a}
is a prefix of
Q
if
j\geq|S|
and
Q=S_{i,\ldots,j}
otherwise. Finally, in order to deal with the final Lock
4
, we distinguished two types of shield depending on the fact that
S
crosses glue column
c
or not.

Definition 8
.

Consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, with
s
as the index of a glue visible on glue column
c
in
P
and
f
as the index of its first glue on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Consider a shield
S
of
P
on glue column
c
at position
s\leq a\leq f
. Shield
S
is a
full
shield if and only if
w_{SP_{a}}\leq c
otherwise
S
is called an
half
shield.

7.2
Protected glues

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
. Also consider
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
. Now, the aim of this section is to
protect
the glues of this decomposition. A notion which can be seen as a generalization of pseudo-visibility.

To do so, we will have to consider three kinds of
protected
glues. The first one is when the glue is visible in
P
on glue column
c
, see Subsection
7.2.1
. The second one is when a glue is protected by a full-shield, see Subsection
7.2.2
. The last one is when a glue is protected by an half-shield, see Subsection
7.2.3
. For each kind of protected glue, we will define a
category
(which is either “main” or “backup”), a
type
(belonging to the set of tile types
T
), a
protected area
(which is included into the area
\mathcal{C}
of Definition
7
) and a
practical form
(which is a path included into the protected area and which is a subassembly of
\gamma
) and a
restrained area
(whose definition relies on the practical form and which is included into the protected area). In order to achieve this goal, we introduce here the
practical form
of
P
which can be defined conjointly for the three types of protected glue:

Definition 9
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
, consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, and let
f
be the index of its first glue on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Also consider
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
to be the upward and downward decomposition of
P
into dominant arcs on glue column
c
and take an index
p
such that
p=m_{i}
or
p=b_{i}-1
. Suppose that an area called the protected area (defined later and differently in the two sub-cases) of
p
is defined. We define the set of paths
{\bf{P}}[{p}]
such that it contains a path
Q
if and only if:

•

Q_{0}=P_{p}
,
Q_{1}=P_{p+1}
,
Q_{|Q|-2}=P_{f}
and
Q_{|Q|-1}=P_{f+1}
;

•

Q_{1,\ldots,|Q|-2}
is in the protected area of
p
;

•

P_{0,\ldots,p-1}Q
is a producible path whose last tile is the easternmost one.

•

Q_{|Q|-1}
is the only tile of
Q
on column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5
.

Let
R
be the path such that
P_{p}R
is the rightmost (resp. leftmost) priority path of this set,
R
is called the
practical form
of
P
at index
p
.

We will give examples and illustrations for these notions for the three different kinds of protected glues.

7.2.1
The visible glue is protected

Definition 10

(Notations for a visible glue)
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
f
be the index of its first glue on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Also consider
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
.

We remind that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
is the glue visible from the south (resp. north) in
P
and we consider it as protected by default, see Figure
46
. It is categorized as a “main” glue, its type is the type of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
, its protected area
\mathcal{P}_{m_{0}}
is
\mathcal{C}
the right side (resp. left side) of the area delimited by
l^{m_{0}}
,
P_{m_{0},\ldots,f+1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and going south (resp. north). Let
R
be the practical form of
P
at index
m_{0}
. In this case,
R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{0}}
and we define the restrained area
\mathcal{R}_{m_{0}}
as the right side (resp. left side) of the area delimited by
l^{m_{0}}
,
P_{m_{0}}R
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and going south (resp. north). This restrained area is inside
\mathcal{C}
, see Figure
46
.

Variants of the two following lemmas are required for each kind of protected glue. We remind that the final goal, explained in Subsections
7.2.2
and
7.2.3
, is to combine two protected glues to find a new one. To achieve this goal, Lemma
20
will allow us to copy and pasted the practical form of one protected glue into the restrained area of the other one (as in Subsection
3.5
of the roadmap). Later in the proof, a third protected glue may be required to conclude (if the two protected glues are southern glues then the third one will be a northern one and vice versa). Then, Lemma
21
allow us to use efficiently this third protected glue.

Lemma 20
.

Consider a path
P
and the notations
10
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{m_{0}}
be the protected area of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
and let
R
the practical form of
P
at index
m_{0}
. Consider a path
Q
such that
Q_{0}=R_{0}
. If
P_{m_{0}}Q
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
P_{m_{0}}R
and if there exist
0\leq u\leq v\leq|Q|-1
such that
R_{0,\ldots,u}
is the largest common prefix between
R
and
Q
and such that
Q_{0,\ldots,v}
intersects with
R_{u+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
, then
Q_{0,\ldots,v}
intersects with the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
or
e_{Q_{0,\ldots,v}}>\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
.

Proof.

Consider a path
Q
such that
Q_{0}=R_{0}
and let
0\leq u\leq|Q|-1
such that
Q_{0,\ldots,u}
is the largest common prefix between
R
and
Q
, see Figure
47
. If
Q_{u+1,\ldots,|Q|-1}
intersects with
R
then we can define
v^{\prime}=\max\{i:\mathrm{pos}(R_{i})\text{ is occupied by a tile of }Q\}
, let
u\leq v\leq|Q|-1
such that
Q_{v}=R_{v^{\prime}}
. By definition of
v
and
v^{\prime}
,
Q_{0,\ldots,v}R_{v^{\prime}+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is a path. Now, if
Q_{0,\ldots,v}
does not intersect the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
, if
x_{Q_{0,\ldots,v}}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and if
P_{m_{0}}Q
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
P_{m_{0}}R
then
Q_{0,\ldots,v}
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{0}}
. Thus
P_{0,\ldots,m_{0}}Q_{0,\ldots,v}
is producible and we have
Q_{v}=R_{v^{\prime}}
since the tile assembly system is directed. Then
P_{0,\ldots,m_{0}}Q_{0,\ldots,v}R_{v^{\prime}+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is a producible path and if
u<v
then this path is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
P_{m_{g}}R
and satisfies all properties of
9
. This is a contradiction of the definition of
R
.

Lemma 21
.

Consider a path
P
, the notations
10
and the index
0\leq\ell\leq|P|-1
of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. If there exists a path
B
such that
B
is in the restrained area
\mathcal{R}_{m_{0}}
of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
,
B
is a subassembly of
\gamma
,
w_{B}>c
,
B_{0}
is a tile of
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
then
B
does not intersect with
P_{m_{0}+1,\ldots,\ell}
.

Proof.

If
B
intersects with
P_{m_{0}+1,\ldots,\ell}
then let
B_{0,\ldots,v}
be the shortest prefix of
B
which intersects with
P_{m_{0}+1,\ldots,\ell}
, see Figure
48
. Let
B_{u,\ldots,v}
be the shortest suffix of
B_{0,\ldots,v}
which intersects with
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
. Since,
w_{B}>c
then there exist
i\leq j<k
such that
P_{i,\ldots,k}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and
P_{j}=B_{v}
(since
B
is a subassembly of
\gamma
). Since
B_{u,\ldots,v}
is a subassembly of
\gamma
and in
\mathcal{R}_{m_{0}}
then there is a contradiction with Corollary
3
since
P
is canonical for glue column
c
.

P_{b_{0}}

B_{v}

. This dominant arc is

Note that, for the special case where the width of

into dominant arcs.

7.2.2
Glues protected by a full-shield

Along this subsection, we will use the following notations:

Definition 11

(Notations for a glue protected by a full-shield)
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
f
be the index of its first glue on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Let
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
and let
(b^{i})_{0\leq i\leq t+1}
be the frontiers of this decomposition. Let
S
be a southern (resp. northern) full-shield of
P
on column
c
at index
m_{0}<a\leq f
. We denote by
\mathcal{C}
the area delimited by
l^{m_{0}}
,
P_{m_{0},\ldots,f+1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{{f+1}})
and going south (resp. north).

Lemma 22
.

Consider the notations
11
. If
S
is a full-shield then, there exist
0\leq e\leq|S|-1
and
0<g\leq t
such that the
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})

12

If
e=|S|-1

by abuse of notation.
intersects the frontier
b^{g}
and
x_{S_{0,\ldots,e}}>c

Proof.

By definition of a full-shield, we have
x_{SP_{a}}\leq c
, see Figure

. Thus, we can define
e=\min\{i:\mathrm{glue}(S_{i}S_{i+1})\text{ is on glue column $c$}\}
. Then, by definition of
e
, we have
x_{S_{e}}=c+0.5
and
x_{S_{e+1}}=c-0.5
. Now, the workspace
\mathcal{C}
can be decomposed into its east side
\mathcal{E}
and the interiors

for all

. Since

then
\mathrm{pos}(S_{e})
is in
\mathcal{E}
. However,
x_{S_{e+1}}\leq c
and

thus
\mathrm{pos}(S_{e+1})
belongs to
\mathcal{I}_{g}
for some
0<g\leq t
. Then,
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
must intersect with
b^{g}
.

Thanks to Lemma

0\leq e\leq|S|-1
and

is defined as the finite area delimited by:

•

•

S_{0,\ldots,e+1}

\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
may be included into the interior of the arc
SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
but this is not always the case.
Remark that
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}
is not inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
. Indeed,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
points east and thus
P_{m_{g}}
is not inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
. Moreover,
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}
cannot intersect with
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,f+1}
(since
P
is self-avoiding), the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
(since this segment is part of the frontier
b^{g}
),
S_{0,\ldots,e}
(since, by definition,
S
does not intersect
P_{0,\ldots,f+1}
and is in
\mathcal{C}
) and the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{0}S_{1})
(since
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}
has no tile on column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5
by Lemma
11
). This remark implies the following fact:

Fact 8
.

Consider the notations
11
and
0<g\leq t
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is protected by the full-shield
S
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
be its protected area.
Consider a path
Q
such that
Q
is inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
and such that
Q_{0}=P_{m_{g}+1}
then
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}Q
is a producible path.

Moreover, the following lemma shows that the beginning
S_{0,\ldots,e}
of the full-shield
S
must be avoided by any path
Q
growing inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
.

Lemma 23
.

Consider the notations
11
,
0<g\leq t
and
0\leq e\leq|S|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is protected by the beginning
S_{0,\ldots,e}
of the full-shield
S
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
be its protected area.
There exists no path
Q
such that
Q
is inside the area
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
,
Q_{0}=P_{m_{g}+1}
and
Q
intersects with
S_{0,\ldots,e}
.

Proof.

For the sake of contradiction suppose that such a path
Q
exists (see Figure
51
), then by Fact
8
,
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}Q
is a producible path. Let
A
be the arc
SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
. Firstly, consider the case where
a\leq m_{g}
, see Figure
52
. In this case,
P_{m_{g}+1}
is inside the interior
\mathcal{I}_{A}
of arc
A
and let
0\leq j\leq|Q|-1
be such that
Q_{0,\ldots,j}
in the shortest prefix of
Q
which intersects with
S
(by hypothesis, this index is correctly defined) and let
0\leq i<j
be such that
Q_{i,\ldots,j}
is the shortest suffix of
Q_{0,\ldots,j}
which intersects with
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,f+1}
(by hypothesis, this index is correctly defined since
Q_{0}=P_{{m_{g}}+1}
). Let
m_{g}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq f
be such that
P_{i^{\prime}}=Q_{i}
and
0\leq j^{\prime}\leq|S|-1
be such that
S_{j^{\prime}}=Q_{j}
then
A^{\prime}=S_{0,\ldots,j^{\prime}-1}\text{rev}({Q_{i,\ldots,j}})P_{i^{\prime}+1,\ldots,f+1}
is a simple path
13
13

13

\text{rev}({Q})
designates the path
Q
in reverse: the first tile becomes the first one and vice versa.
. Since
Q
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
then
e_{Q}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and thus
A^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|A^{\prime}|-1}
is inside
\mathcal{I}_{A}
and is a negative arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Moreover,
A^{\prime}
does not pass by
P_{m_{g}}
since this tile is not inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
. The existence of this arc contradicts the definition of a shield, since
S
should be the prefix of
\min(\mathcal{I}_{A})
.

), its interior
\mathcal{I}_{A}
is the colored area (orange and yellow). The tiles of
P_{m_{1},\ldots,a-1}
are in light blue. The path
Q
must intersect
S_{h+1,\ldots,|S|-1}P_{a}
to reach
S_{0,\ldots,e}
. The white and red dots correspond to the path
Q
of Figure
51
. By definition of a shield,
SP_{a}
should be a prefix of
\min(\mathcal{I}_{A})
. Nevertheless, the red dots (corresponding to
Q_{i,\ldots,j}
) connect two tiles of
S
(in this example) allowing to take a shortcut and to avoid the light blue dots (
S_{h}
is among these dots). This is a contradiction which means that
Q
cannot exist.

∎

Let
R
be the practical form of
P
at index
m_{g}
, see Figure
54
. Lemma
23
implies that
R
does not intersect
S_{0,\ldots,e}
. Then, we can define
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
as the
restrained protected area
delimited by:

•

P_{m_{g}}R
,

•

the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
,

•

S_{0,\ldots,e+1}
,

•

the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{0}S_{1})
.

This area is included into
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
.

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{\ell}
S_{0}
P_{f}
S_{e}
P_{m_{1}}

Figure 54:
The colored area (light blue and blue) corresponds to the protected area
\mathcal{P}_{m_{1}}
of the path
P
from Figure
50
. The red dots and tiles correspond to
R
, the practical form of
P
at index
m_{1}
, which is the rightmost priority path from

to

which is inside in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{1}}
. The restrained area
\mathcal{R}_{m_{1}}
is in blue.

Variants of the two following lemmas are required for each kind of protected glue, see Subsection
7.2.1
dedicated to visible glues.

Lemma 24
.

Consider the notations
11
,
0<g\leq t
and
0\leq e\leq|S|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is protected by the beginning
S_{0,\ldots,e}
of the full-shield
S
. Let
R
be the practical form of
P
at index
m_{g}
and let
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
be its restrained protected area. Consider a path
Q
such that
Q_{0}=R_{0}
. If
P_{m_{g}}Q
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
P_{m_{g}}R
either
Q
is in
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
or
e_{Q}>\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
or
Q
intersects with the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
.

Proof.

Let
0\leq v\leq|Q|-1
such that
Q_{0,\ldots,v}
is the largest prefix of
Q
which is inside
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
, see Figure
55
. Let
0\leq u\leq|Q|-1
such that
Q_{0,\ldots,u}
is the largest common prefix between
Q
and
R
. Since
P_{m_{g}}Q
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
P_{m_{g}}R
then
u\leq v
. By Lemma
23
,
Q_{0,\dots,v}
cannot intersect
S_{0,\ldots,e}
. If
Q_{0,\dots,v}
intersects with
R_{u+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
then let
z=\max\{i:\mathrm{pos}(R_{i})\text{ is occupied by a tile of }Q_{0,\ldots,v}\}
and
u<z^{\prime}\leq v
such that
P_{z}=Q_{z^{\prime}}
. Then,
Q_{0,\dots,z^{\prime}}R_{z+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is in
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
and
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}Q_{0,\dots,z^{\prime}}R_{z+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is producible and
P_{m_{g}}Q_{0,\dots,z^{\prime}}R_{z+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
P_{m_{g}}R
and satisfies all properties of definition
9
. This is a contradiction of the definition of
R
. Thus, if
v<|Q|-1
, then either
Q_{v+1}
is on column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5
or
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{v}Q_{v+1})
is on the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
.

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{\ell}
S_{0}
P_{f}
S_{e}
P_{m_{1}}

Figure 55:
Proof of Lemma
24
based on the example of Figure
54
. The orange dots represent a path
Q
which is inside
\mathcal{R}_{m_{1}}
, whose first tile binds with
P_{m_{1}}
and which intersects with
R
after turning right of
R
. In this case, there is a contradiction since
R
would not the rightmost priority path from
P_{m_{1}+1}
to
P_{f+1}
which is inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{1}}
.

∎

Lemma 25
.

Consider the notations
11
,
0<g\leq t
and
0\leq e\leq|S|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is protected by the beginning
S_{0,\ldots,e}
of the full-shield
S
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
be the protected area of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
. Let
0\leq\ell\leq|P|-1
be the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. If there exists a path
B
such that
B
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
,
B
is a subassembly of the terminal assembly
\gamma
,
w_{B}>c
,
B_{0}
is a tile of
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
then
B
does not intersect with
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,\ell}
or
S_{0,\ldots,e}
.

Proof.

If
B
intersects with
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,\ell}
then let
B_{0,\ldots,v}
be the shortest prefix of
B
which intersects with
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,\ell}
, see Figure
56
. Let
B_{u,\ldots,v}
be the shortest suffix of
B_{0,\ldots,v}
which intersects with
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
. Since,
w_{B}>c
then there exists
i<j<k
such that
P_{i,\ldots,k}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and
P_{j}=B_{v}
(since
B
is a subassembly of
\gamma
). Since
B_{u,\ldots,v}
is a subassembly of the terminal assembly of
\gamma
and in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
then there is a contradiction with Corollary
3
since
P
is canonical for glue column
c
.

If
B
intersects with
S_{0,\ldots,e}
then let
B_{0,\ldots,v}
be the shortest prefix of
B
which intersects with
S_{0,\ldots,e}
. Let
B_{u,\ldots,v}
be the shortest suffix of
B_{0,\ldots,v}
which intersects with
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
. There exists
\ell+1\leq u^{\prime}\leq f
such that
P_{u^{\prime}}=B_{u}
. By the previous paragraph,
B_{u,\ldots,v}
does not intersect with
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,\ell}
. Thus
P_{m_{g}+1,\ldots,u^{\prime}-1}B_{u,\ldots,v}
is path which is in inside
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
. This path contradicts Lemma
23
.

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{\ell}
S_{0}
P_{f}
S_{e}
P_{m_{1}}

Figure 56:
Proof of Lemma
25
based on the example of Figure
54
. The orange dots represent a path
B
which is inside
\mathcal{R}_{m_{0}}
(the blue area), such that
w_{B}>c
and which intersects with
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
and
P_{m_{1}+1,\ldots,\ell}
. In this case
B
must intersect with a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
contradicting Corollary
3
. This dominant arc is
P_{m_{1},\ldots,b_{1}}
in this example and is represented by the orange tiles. If
B
intersects with
S_{0,\ldots,e}
instead of
P_{m_{1}+1,\ldots,\ell}
then we have a contradiction by Lemma
23
.

∎

7.2.3
Glues protected by an half-shield

Along this subsection, we will use the following notations:

Definition 12

(Notations for a glue protected by an half-shield)
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider a path
P
which is canonical for some glue column
c
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
and let
f
be the index of its first glue on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Let
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
and let
(b^{i})_{0\leq i\leq t+1}
be the frontiers of this decomposition. Let
S
be an southern (resp. northen) half-shield of
P
on glue column
c
at index
m_{0}<a\leq f
. Let
A=SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
.

Lemma 26
.

Consider the notations
12
, there exists
0\leq g<t
such that
m_{g}<a<b_{g}
.

Proof.

In this case, consider
\mathcal{C}
the area defined by the right (resp. left) side of the curve made of
l^{m_{0}}
,
P_{m_{0},\ldots,f+1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{f+1})
and going south (resp. north), see Figure
57
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be its east side (the intersection of
\mathcal{C}
with the half-plane of equation
x\geq c
). By definition of an half-shield,
w_{SP_{a}}>c
then
P_{a}
is in
\mathcal{E}
. Thus, either
m_{t}<a\leq f
or there exists
0\leq g<t
such that
m_{g}<a<b_{g}
. If
g<t
then the lemma is true. Otherwise, we have
m_{t}<a\leq f
. In this case, remind that
m_{t}
is the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. Thus,
w_{P_{a,\ldots,f+1}}>c
. Then, we would have
w_{A}>c
which contradicts the definition of a shield.
∎

Thanks to Lemma
26
, we can now assume that there exists
0\leq g<t
such that
m_{g}<a<b_{g}
, see Figure
57
. In this case, we say that the
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{g}-1}P_{b_{g}})
is
protected
by the half-shield
S
. The category of this protected glue is a “backup”. The protected area
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
of
b_{g}
is
\mathcal{I}_{A}
, the interior of the hole
A
.

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{\ell}
S_{0}
P_{f}
P_{m_{1}}
P_{b_{1}}
P_{a}

Figure 57:
The half-shield
S
(the dark blue dots) of
P
at position
a
protects the
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{1}-1}P_{b_{1}})
. The arc
SP_{a,\ldots,f+1}
is made of
S
and the light blue tiles. The interior of this arc is the protected area
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1}}
which is the colored area (light blue and blue). The path in red dots correspond to
R
, the practical form of
P
at index
b_{1}
, which is the rightmost priority path from
P_{b_{1}}
to
P_{f}
and which is inside
\mathcal{P}_{b_{1}}
. Note that we only represent the tiles of
R
which differ from the ones of
P
. The restrained area
\mathcal{R}_{b_{1}}
is in blue.

Lemma 27
.

Consider the notations
12
, let
R
be the practical form of
P
at index
b_{g}-1
. Then
SP_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}R
is a negative upward (resp. downward) arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
included into
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
(apart from its first and last tiles).

Proof.

By definition of a practical form,
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}-1}R
is a producible path and then
P_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
does not intersect with
R
. Moreover
R
is inside
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
. If
R
intersects with
S
then there is a contradiction with the hypothesis that
SP_{a}
is prefix the minimal arc of
\mathcal{I}_{A}
(by a reasoning similar to the first case of Lemma
23
for example). Thus
R
and
S
do not intersect and
SP_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}R
is a negative arc upward (resp. downward) arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
included into
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
(apart from its first and last tiles).
∎

Lemma
27
allows us to defined the restrained area
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
of
b_{g}
as the interior of the arc
SP_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}R
. This area is included into
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
, see Figure
57
.

The following lemma is the equivalent of Lemma
21
and Lemma
25
for a backup glue protected by an half-shield. As explained in Subsection
7.2.1
, this lemma will be useful in Subsection
7.3
. Indeed, combining two main southern protected glues may require a third northern protected glue. This northern glue may be protected by an half-shield.

Lemma 28
.

Consider the notations
12
, let
0\leq\ell\leq|P|-1
be the index of the last glue of
P
on glue column
c
. If there exists a path
B
such that
B
is in
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
,
B
is a subassembly of
\gamma
,
w_{B}>c
,
B_{0}
is a tile of
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
then
B
does not intersect with
P_{a,\ldots,\ell}
or
S
.

Proof.

If
B
intersects with
P_{a,\ldots,\ell}
then let
B_{0,\ldots,v}
be the shortest prefix of
B
which intersects with
P_{a,\ldots,\ell}
. Let
B_{u,\ldots,v}
be the shortest suffix of
B_{0,\ldots,v}
which intersects with
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
. Since,
w_{B}>c
then there exists
i\leq j<k
such that
P_{i,\ldots,k}
is a dominant arc of
P
on glue column
c
and
P_{j}=B_{v}
(since
B
is a subassembly of
\gamma
). Since
B_{u,\ldots,v}
is a subassembly of
\gamma
and in
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
then there is a contradiction with Corollary
3
since
P
is canonical for glue column
c
.

If
B
intersects with
S
then let
B_{0,\ldots,v}
be the shortest prefix of
B
which intersects with
S
. Let
B_{u,\ldots,v}
be the shortest suffix of
B_{0,\ldots,v}
which intersects with
P_{\ell+1,\ldots,f}
. There exists
\ell+1\leq u^{\prime}\leq f
such that
P_{u^{\prime}}=B_{u}
. By the previous paragraph,
B_{u,\ldots,v}
does not intersect with
P_{a,\ldots,\ell}
. Thus
P_{a,\ldots,u^{\prime}-1}B_{u,\ldots,v}
is path which is in inside
\mathcal{P}_{b_{g}}
. We obtain a contradiction by a reasoning similar to the first case of Lemma
27
.
∎

With this result, we may proceed to explain how to combine two “main” protected glues to find a new protected one. Concerning the equivalence of Lemma
20
and Lemma
24
, the whole Subsection
7.4
is dedicated to this result. Indeed, achieving this goal is far more complex for a glue protected by an half-shield.

7.3
Combining two identical “main” protected glues

Along this subsection, we will use the following notations:

Definition 13

(Notations for combining two “main” glues)
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider two paths
P
and
Q
which are canonical for some glue columns
c
and
c^{\prime}
respectively with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c<c^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
.
Let
0\leq f<|P|-1
be the index of the first glue of
P
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
.
Let
0\leq f^{\prime}<|Q|-1
be the index of the first glue of
Q
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})
.
Let
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
.
Let
0\leq g\leq t
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is a protected “main” glue of
P
. Let
R
be the practical form of
P
at index
m_{g}
and let
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
be its restrained protected area.
Consider the index
0\leq p\leq|Q|-1
of a protected “main” glue of the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
Q
into dominants arcs on glue column
c^{\prime}
.
Let
R^{\prime}
be the practical form of
Q
at index
p
and let
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
be its restrained protected area.
Suppose that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g+1}})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
have the same type and let
\overrightarrow{v}=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{pos}(P_{m_{g}})\mathrm{pos}(Q_{p})}
.

As an example for the path
P
, we will first use the path
P
of Figure
58
and for the path
Q
, we will use the example from Figure
54
. The following lemma corresponds to the reasoning of the roadmap where we explain that only the first case of Fact
3
is possible.

P_{\ell}
S_{0}
P_{f}
S_{e}
P_{m_{g}}

Figure 58:
A path
P
which is canonical for glue column
c
. Only
P_{m_{g},\ldots,f+1}
is represented (the orange tiles). In this example, the main southern
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is protected by the beginning
S_{0,\ldots,e}
of the shield
S
. Only
S_{0,\ldots,e}
is represented by the blue dots. The colored area (orange and yellow) represents the protected area
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
. The practical form
R
of
P
at index
m_{g}
corresponds to the yellow dots. The restrained area
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
of
P
is in yellow.

Lemma 29
.

Consider the notations
13
,
R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v}
is not inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we suppose that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
are southern main glues.
For the sake of contradiction, suppose that
R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
, see Figure
59
. We remind that by definition of
R_{0}
, we have
R_{0}=P_{m_{g}+1}
. Also, by definition of
\overrightarrow{v}
,
\mathrm{pos}(R_{0})+\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(Q_{p}+1)
. By hypothesis,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g+1}})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
have the same type thus
R_{0}+\overrightarrow{v}
and
Q_{p}
binds together. Then by Fact
8
,
Q_{0,\ldots,p}(R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v})
is a producible path.

Q_{s^{\prime}}
Q_{n^{\prime}}
Q_{\ell^{\prime}}
S^{\prime}_{0}
Q_{f^{\prime}}
S^{\prime}_{e^{\prime}}
Q_{p}

Figure 59:
Proof of Lemma
29
, part (1/3): a path
Q
which is canonical for glue column
c^{\prime}
(this example comes from Figure
54
). The yellow diamonds correspond to the practical form
R
of the path
P
at index
m_{g}
from Figure
58
translated by
\overrightarrow{v}
. We suppose that
R_{0}+\overrightarrow{v}
can bind with
Q_{p}
. We consider the case where
R+\overrightarrow{v}
(except for its last tile) is inside the restrained area
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
(the blue area).

Firstly, suppose that there is no intersection between
R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}
and
P_{f+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
. By definition of
R
, we have
R_{|R|-2}=P_{f}
then
R_{|R|-2}
and
P_{f+1}
binds together (which also true for
R_{|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v}
and
P_{f+1}+\overrightarrow{v}
). Let
0\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq|Q|-1
be the index of the last glue of
Q
on glue column
c^{\prime}
. Since
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
is on glue column
c^{\prime}
, we have
p\leq\ell^{\prime}
. Then, it is possible to apply Lemma
12
on
Q_{0,\ldots,p}(R+\overrightarrow{v})
and
P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v}
(the bound is satisfied since
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})=\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+(c^{\prime}-c)
, see Fact
4
). We obtain that
Q_{0,\ldots,p}(R+\overrightarrow{v})(P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v})
is a subassembly of
\gamma
. Nevertheless, since
P
is canonical for glue column
c
then by definition
x_{P_{|P|-1}}=e_{\gamma}
and then
x_{P_{|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v}}>e_{\gamma}
. This is a contradiction.

Secondly, suppose that there are some intersections between
R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}
and
P_{f+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
, see Figure
60
. Since,
P
and
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}R
are producible path, then all intersections between
R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}
and
P_{f+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
are agreements. Let

u=\max\{i:\mathrm{pos}(P_{i})\text{ is occupied by a tile of }R\}

and let
u^{\prime}
such that
R_{u^{\prime}}=P_{u}
. We are in case where
u\geq f+1
and by definition of
u
,
R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
is a path. For the sake of contradiction suppose that
R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
is more left-priority than
R
then
P_{u+1}
is inside a finite area delimited by
R
and
P_{m_{g},\ldots,f+1}
, see Figure
60
. Nevertheless, since
x_{P_{|P|-1}}=e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5
and
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5=\max\{e_{R},e_{P_{m_{g},\ldots,f+1}}\}
then
P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
must intersect with either
P_{m_{g},\ldots,f+1}
(which cannot occur since
P
is self-avoiding) or
R
(which cannot occur by definition of
u
). This is a contradiction and then
R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1}
is more right-priority than
R
. Since
(R+\overrightarrow{v})
is inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
then
Q_{p}(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1})+\overrightarrow{v}
is more right-priority than
Q_{p}R^{\prime}
, see Figure
61
. Note that
u\geq f
and then
w_{P_{u,\ldots,|P|-1}}>c
(implying that
w_{P_{u,\ldots,|P|-1}}+\overrightarrow{v}>c^{\prime}
). Then, by Lemma
20
(if
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
is visible from the south) or Lemma
24
(if
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
is not visible from the south),
(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1})+\overrightarrow{v}
can leave
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
only by intersecting with glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})
. Let
u\leq v\leq|P|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{v}P_{v+1})
is the first glue of
(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1})+\overrightarrow{v}
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})
. then
(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,v})+\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
and
Q_{0,\ldots,p}(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,v})+\overrightarrow{v}
is producible by Fact
8
. We can now apply Lemma
12
on
Q_{0,\ldots,p}(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,v+1})+\overrightarrow{v}
and
(P_{v+2,\ldots,|P|-1})+\overrightarrow{v}
and we obtain that
Q_{0,\ldots,p}(R_{0,\ldots,u^{\prime}}P_{u+1,\ldots,|P|-1})+\overrightarrow{v}
is producible. Nevertheless, since
P
is canonical for glue column
c
then by definition
x_{P_{|P|-1}}=e_{\gamma}
and then
x_{P_{|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v}}>e_{\gamma}
. This is a contradiction.

. As long as this path stays inside
