∎

The following lemma corresponds to the reasoning of the roadmap where we explore the consequence of the first case of Fact
3
. Here, this is the first step toward assembling a new shield for path
P
.

Lemma 30
.

Consider the notations
13
, then
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}(R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible. Moreover, there exists
0\leq a\leq|R|-1
such that
(\text{rev}({R^{\prime}_{a,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-1}})-\overrightarrow{v})R_{a+1,|R|-1}
is a negative upward (resp. downward) arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and its interior is included into
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we suppose that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
are southern main glues.
We remind that by definition of
R_{0}
, we have
R_{0}=P_{m_{g}+1}
. Also, by definition of
\overrightarrow{v}
,
\mathrm{pos}(R_{0})+\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(Q_{p}+1)
. By hypothesis,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g+1}})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
have the same type thus
R_{0}+\overrightarrow{v}
and
Q_{p}
binds together. Since the tile assembly system is directed, we have
R_{0}+\overrightarrow{v}=R^{\prime}_{0}
. By Lemma
29
,
(R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v})
is not inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
. Thus
(R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v})
cannot be a prefix of
R^{\prime}
.

For the sake of contradiction, suppose that
Q_{p}(R+\overrightarrow{v})
is more right-priority than
Q_{p}R^{\prime}
, see Figure
62
for a similar reasoning to show that
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
does not cross the segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
. Consider
v
such that
(R_{0,\ldots,v}+\overrightarrow{v})
is the largest prefix of
(R+\overrightarrow{v})
which is inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
. Since
(R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v})
is not inside
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
then
v<|R|-2
. Moreover, since
(R_{0,\ldots,|R|-2}+\overrightarrow{v})<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})
, then by Lemma
24
or Lemma
20
,
\mathrm{glue}(R_{v}R_{v+1})
is on the vertical ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
and going south. Note that this glue should point west to leave
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
. Then, consider
0\leq u\leq v
such that
(R_{u,\ldots,v+1}+\overrightarrow{v})
is a positive arc on column
c^{\prime}
. If
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
is visible from the south then
\mathrm{glue}(R_{v}R_{v+1})
is on the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
. The
\mathrm{glue}(R_{u}R_{u+1})
cannot be on this glue ray by minimality of
v
and
(R_{u,\ldots,v+1}+\overrightarrow{v})
must be a downward arc. If
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
is not visible from the south then it is protected by the beginning
S^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,e^{\prime}}
of a shield
S^{\prime}
. Then,
(R_{u,\ldots,v+1}+\overrightarrow{v})
must be a downward arc to avoid
S^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,e^{\prime}}
. In all cases,
(R_{u,\ldots,v+1}+\overrightarrow{v})
is a downward arc. Then
R_{u,\ldots,v+1}
is a positive downward arc on column
c
such that
y_{R_{v}}<y_{P_{m_{g}}}\leq y_{R_{u}}
.
If
g=0
then
R_{u,\ldots,v+1}
would intersect the glue ray of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{0}}P_{m_{0}+1})
contradicting the definition of
R
. If
g>0
then there exists a shield
S
and
0\leq e\leq|S|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is protected by the end
S_{0,\ldots,e}
of shield
S
. In this case, either
R_{u,\ldots,v+1}
intersects
S_{0,\ldots,e}
(which is a contradiction by Lemma
23
) or
R_{u,\ldots,v+1}
leaves
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
by crossing the vertical segment between
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(S_{e}S_{e+1})
(contradicting the definition of
R
).

The last possible case is that
R^{\prime}
is more right-priority than
(R+\overrightarrow{v})
, see Figure
62
. This means that
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
is more right-priority than
R
. Remark that the paths
R
and
R^{\prime}
and the protected areas
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
have the same properties. Then with the same kind of reasoning, it is possible to prove that
R^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-2}-\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
. By Fact
8
, we obtain that
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}(R^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-2}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible. Since
x_{R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}}=\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5
and
e_{P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}(R^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-2}-\overrightarrow{v})}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
then
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}}(R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v})
is also producible. Consider
0\leq a\leq|R|-1
such that
R_{0,\ldots,a}
is the largest common prefix between
R
and
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
. By Lemma
20
(if
g=0
) or Lemma
24
(if
g>0
),
R_{a+1,\ldots,|R|-2}
and
R^{\prime}_{a+1,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-2}-\overrightarrow{v}
do not intersect. Then
\mathrm{pos}(R_{|R|-2})\neq\mathrm{pos}(R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-2})-\overrightarrow{v}
and
y_{R_{|R|-2}}>y_{R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-2}}-\overrightarrow{v}
(since
x_{R_{|R|-2}}=x_{R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-2}-\overrightarrow{v}}=\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)-0.5
), then we have
\mathrm{pos}(R_{|R|-1})\neq\mathrm{pos}(R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-1})-\overrightarrow{v}
. Then,
R_{a+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
and
R^{\prime}_{a+1,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
do not intersect. To conclude, the path
\text{rev}({R^{\prime}_{a,\ldots,|R^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}})R_{a+1,|R|-1}
is a negative upward arc of column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and its interior is included into
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
.

P_{\ell}
P_{f}
R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}

. Only

∎

In Lemma
30
, a negative upward arc on column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
which is inside
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
was found. Now, we focus on the minimum arc in the interior of this arc.

Lemma 31
.

Consider the notations
13
, there exists an upward (resp. downward) minimum arc
A^{\prime}
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
such that its interior
\mathcal{I}_{A^{\prime}}
is included into
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
, such that
A^{\prime}
is a subassembly of
\gamma
, such that
A^{\prime}_{0}=Q_{f^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v}
and
A^{\prime}_{|A^{\prime}|-1}=P_{f+1}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
30
, there exists a negative upward (resp. downward) arc
B
of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
such
B_{0}=R^{\prime}_{|R^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}=Q_{f^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v}
and
B_{|B|-1}=R_{|R|-1}=P_{f+1}
. Moreover,
B
is a subassembly of
\gamma
and its interior is included into
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
. The path
A^{\prime}
defined as
\min(\mathcal{I}_{B})
satisfies the condition of the lemma.
∎

Under some hypothesis a new shield and a new protected glue can be extracted from the arc
A^{\prime}
.

Lemma 32
.

Consider the notations
13
and the arc
A^{\prime}
of Lemma
31
. If
w_{A^{\prime}}\leq c
, then either there exists
g\leq i<t
such that
b_{i}
is protected or there exists
g<i\leq t
such that
m_{i}
is protected.

Proof.

By definition, we have
A^{\prime}_{|A^{\prime}|-2}=P_{f}
, then
A^{\prime}
intersects with
P_{m_{g},\ldots,f+1}
. Let
S^{\prime}
be the largest prefix of
A^{\prime}
such that
S^{\prime}
does not intersect
P_{m_{g},\ldots,f+1}
.
Note that,
S^{\prime}
does not intersect with
P_{0,\ldots,m_{g}-1}
since the interior of
A^{\prime}
is in
\mathcal{R}_{m_{g}}
.
Also,
S^{\prime}
contains at least
A^{\prime}_{0}
since
x_{A^{\prime}_{0}}=\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)+0.5
,
\mathrm{pos}(A^{\prime}_{0})\neq\mathrm{pos}(P_{f+1})
and
e_{P_{m_{g},\ldots,f}}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
.
Since
S^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|S^{\prime}|-2}
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
and by definition of
S^{\prime}
there exists
m_{g}<a^{\prime}\leq f
such that
S^{\prime}P_{a^{\prime}}
is a prefix of
A^{\prime}
. Then,
S^{\prime}P_{a^{\prime},\ldots,f+1}
is also a negative upward arc of glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
. Moreover, since
A^{\prime}
is minimum then
A^{\prime}
is inside the interior of the arc
S^{\prime}P_{a^{\prime},\ldots,f+1}
. Then, we have
w_{S^{\prime}P_{a^{\prime},\ldots,f+1}}\leq w_{A^{\prime}}\leq c
. This implies that
S^{\prime}
is a southern shield of
P
on glue column
c
.

We remind that
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
are the upward decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
. Let
(b^{i})_{0\leq i\leq t+1}
be the frontiers of this decomposition. We denote by
\mathcal{C}
the area delimited by
l^{m_{0}}
,
P_{m_{0},\ldots,f+1}
and the ray starting at
\mathrm{glue}(P_{f}P_{{f+1}})
and going south. Now, either
S^{\prime}
is a full-shield but since
S^{\prime}
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
then it cannot intersect
b^{i}
for
i\leq g
, see Figure
63
. Thus, by Lemma
22
there exists
i>g
such that
m_{i}
is protected and the lemma is true. Now, if
S^{\prime}
is an half-shield then
S^{\prime}P_{a^{\prime}}
is in the east side
\mathcal{E}
of
\mathcal{C}
and we have
m_{i}<a^{\prime}<b_{i}
for some
0\leq i<t
, see Figure
64
. Also, since
S^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|S^{\prime}|-1}
is in
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
, then
i\geq g
and the
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{i}}P_{b_{i}+1})
is protected with
b_{i}>m_{g}
.

Lemma 33
.

Consider the notations

and the arc

of Lemma

. If there exists an index

such that the

into dominant arcs on glue column

.

, see Figure

, and we denote by

of glue column

R^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,b}
is the shortest prefix of
R^{\prime}
which intersects with
A^{\prime}+\overrightarrow{v}
. Let
0\leq b^{\prime}\leq|A^{\prime}|-1
such that

and then
T=R^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,b}(A^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}+1,\ldots,|A^{\prime}|-1}+\overrightarrow{v})
is a path and

is a producible path. Note that
P_{f+1}+\overrightarrow{v}=A^{\prime}_{|A^{\prime}|-1}
and
P_{f+1}+\overrightarrow{v}
is on column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})+0.5
. By Lemma
12
, if
P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v}
does not intersect with
T
, then
Q_{0,\ldots,p}T(P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v})
is a producible path. Nevertheless,
x_{P_{|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v}}>e_{\gamma}
and we have a contradiction.

Finally, suppose that there is an intersection between
T
and
(P_{f+2,\ldots,|P|-1}+\overrightarrow{v})
, see Figures

and

. By definition

,

. Thus all intersections between these paths are agreements. Let

, we are in a case where

. Let

such that

. Let

be the index of the first tile of

. If

, then

is inside a finite area delimited by

and

. Nevertheless,

must leave this area but it cannot intersect with

(since

is simple) or

(by definition of

).
Then

is more right-priority than

. If

is inside

then

is producible and by Lemma

,

is producible. Again,

and we have a contradiction.

which intersects with the shield and is in

which is a contradiction by Lemma
23
. All cases lead to contradiction and the lemma is true.

∎

Finally, here is the main result of this section.

Lemma 34
.

Consider the notations
13
, if there exists an index
p^{\prime}\geq p
such that the
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p^{\prime}}Q_{p^{\prime}+1})
is a northern (resp. southern) protected glue (either a “main” or “backup” one) of the downward (resp. upward) decomposition of
Q
into dominant arcs on glue column
c^{\prime}
then either there exists
g\leq i<t
such that
b_{i}
is protected or there exists
g<i\leq t
such that
m_{i}
is protected.

Proof.

By Lemma
31
, there exists an upward (resp. downward) minimum arc
A^{\prime}
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
such that its interior
\mathcal{I}_{A^{\prime}}
is included into
\mathcal{P}_{m_{g}}
, such that
A^{\prime}
is a subassembly of
\gamma
, such that
A^{\prime}_{0}=Q_{f^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v}
and
A^{\prime}_{|A^{\prime}|-1}=P_{f+1}
. Moreover, by Lemma
33
, we have
w_{A^{\prime}}\leq c
. Then by Lemma
32
, the lemma is true.
∎

7.4
Combining two identical “backup” protected glues

Along this subsection, we will use the following notations:

Definition 14

(Notations for combining two “half” glues)
.

Suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
and consider two paths
P
and
Q
which are canonical for some glue columns
c
and
c^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c<c^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
.

•

Let

be the index of the first glue of
P
on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
.

•

Let
0\leq f^{\prime}<|Q|-1
be the index of the first glue of

on glue column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})
.

•

Let
(m_{i})_{0\leq i\leq t}
and
(b_{i})_{0\leq i<t}
the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
P
into dominants arcs on glue column
c
.
Let
0\leq g<t
, a path

and an index

at position

. Let

be the practical form of

at index

be its restrained protected area.

•

Let
0\leq p<|Q|-1
such that
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p-1}Q_{p})
is a “backup” glue of the upward (resp. downward) decomposition of
Q
into dominants arcs on glue column

. This
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p-1}Q_{p})
is protected by the half-shield

at position

. Let

at index

and let
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{p}
its restrained protected area.

Remark that in the Subsection
7.2.3
, we did not define the type of a protected backup glue. At first glance, it seems logical to consider that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{g}}P_{b_{g}+1})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p}Q_{p+1})
should have the same type. Then, we define
\overrightarrow{v}=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{pos}(P_{b_{g}})\mathrm{pos}(Q_{p})}
to try to assemble
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
at the end of
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
. Unfortunately, Figure
69
shows a problematic case. Indeed,
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
may turn right of
R
, stay inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
until intersecting with
P_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
. In this case, a tile of
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
shares the same position as a tile of
P_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
but the two tiles may not share the same type: assembling
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
requires to assemble
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
first. Thus, the growth of
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
can be stopped and we cannot apply the same reasoning as in Subsection
7.3
.

P_{s}
P_{n}
P_{\ell}

, we need to introduce a new notion. The type of a backup glue while not be the type of the protected glue but the type of a

. The index

,

and

and its

-coordinate is

\displaystyle\max\{y:\text{there }

such that

is

\displaystyle\text{on glue column $c$, $y_{P_{i}}=y$ and $y<y_{P_{b_{g}}}$}\}.

\displaystyle\text{(resp. }\min\{y:\text{there }

exists

such that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{i}P_{i+1})
is

\displaystyle\text{on glue column $c$, $y_{P_{i}}=y$ and $y>y_{P_{b_{g}}}$}\}.)

Note that

is correctly defined since
\mathrm{glue}(P_{m_{g}}P_{m_{g}+1})
is on glue column
c
and strictly to the south (resp. north) of the protected
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{g}-1}P_{b_{g}})
14

In some case, we can have

or in other cases, it is possible that

. Nevertheless, these different cases are not relevant for the rest of the proof.
. The
type
of a protected backup glue is the type of
\mathrm{glue}(P_{h}P_{h+1})
. The

of the backup glue is

. From this definition, it follows that

Instead of using equality between visible glues, we will switch in this case to looking for equality between the hidden glue associated with the visible glues,

. Then, the reasoning of Subsection

can be followed to conclude in Subsection

.

7.4.1
Case where
w=w^{\prime}

Consider the notations
15
, when

, the key argument is to remark that
L_{1,\ldots,|L|-1}=L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
.

Lemma 35
.

Consider the notations
15
, If
w=w^{\prime}
then
L_{1,\ldots,|L|-1}=L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
.

Proof.

W. l. o. g., we suppose that
L
is an upward hole
15
15

15

This means that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{g-1}}P_{b_{g}})
and
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{b_{g^{\prime}-1}}Q_{b_{g^{\prime}}})
are southern protected glues.
, see Figure
71
. In this case, the interior
\mathcal{I}_{L}
corresponds to the left side of
L
. Now, let
1\leq u\leq|L|-1
such that
L_{1,\ldots,u}
is the largest common prefix between
L_{1,\ldots,|L|-1}
and
L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
. If
u=|L|-1=|L^{\prime}|-1
then the lemma is true.

For the sake of contradiction suppose that
u<|L|-1
and w.l.o.g., we suppose that
L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
turns left of
L_{1,\ldots,|L|-1}
(the other case is symmetric). Since by hypothesis,
\mathrm{glue}(P_{h}P_{h+1})=\mathrm{glue}(Q_{h^{\prime}}Q_{h^{\prime}+1})-\overrightarrow{v}
and
w=w^{\prime}
then
\mathrm{pos}(L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1})-\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(L_{|L|-1})
.
This remark implies that there is at least one intersection between
L_{u+1,\ldots,|L|-1}
and
L^{\prime}_{u+1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
. Then, we can define
u<v^{\prime}\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
such that
L^{\prime}_{u+1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}
is the shortest prefix of
L^{\prime}_{u+1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
to intersect
L_{u+1,\ldots,|L|-1}
. Let
u<v\leq|L|-1
such that
\mathrm{pos}(L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})-\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(L_{v})
. Remark that the path
L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}
cannot leave
\mathcal{I}_{L}
by crossing the frontier of the hole
L
(otherwise
L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}
would also cross the frontier of the hole
L^{\prime}
which contradicts the definition of a hole) or by crossing through
L
(by definition of
u
and
v^{\prime}
). Then
L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{I}_{L}
. Thus
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible since no tile of
P_{0,\ldots,h}
or the seed
\sigma
is inside
\mathcal{I}_{L}
. In particular,
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v})L_{v+1,\ldots,|L|-1}
is producible, is inside
\mathcal{I}_{L}
and turns left of
P_{0,\ldots,h}L_{1,\ldots,|L|-1}
. This is a contradiction of the definition of
L
as
\min(\mathcal{I}_{L})
.
∎

In particular, this lemma implies:

P_{b_{g}}=L_{|L|-1}=L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}=Q_{p}-\overrightarrow{v}.

Then we can try to grow

from

cannot occurs anymore. Indeed, if
R^{\prime}
and

does not conflict then
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
and
L
does not conflict since

.

.

Consider the notations

Of course there exists a version of this lemma obtained by switching the role of the two paths.

Lemma 37
.

Consider the notations
15
, a path

In the setting of notations

cannot be stopped by a tile of

according to Figure

. Then, we do not need to assemble this part of

if

is assembled first. Thus, the red diamond of Figure

is in fact an agreement with the tile of

and there is no conflict. Moreover, such an agreement would create a contradiction with the priority of

according to Corollary
3
. This last remark allow us to conclude this section by stating a lemma which is an equivalent to Lemma

and Lemma

.

L^{\prime}_{u}-\overrightarrow{v}

). Here
L_{0}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v})L_{v+1,\ldots,|L|-1}
turns left of
L
, is inside
\mathcal{I}_{L}
and is a hole of glue column
c
(its interior is in orange). This contradicts the minimality of
L
.

If
\mathrm{pos}(T_{v})-\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(R_{v^{\prime}})
for some
v^{\prime}>b_{g}+u
, then
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}}(T_{0,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v})R_{v^{\prime}+1,\ldots,f}
is producible. Nevertheless,
(T_{0,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v})R_{v^{\prime}+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
R
which is a contradiction of the definition of
R
.

Now if
\mathrm{pos}(T_{v})-\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(S_{v^{\prime}})
for some
0\leq v^{\prime}\leq|S|-1
, then
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}}(T_{0,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible implying that
T_{v}-\overrightarrow{v}=S_{v^{\prime}}
. Nevertheless,
S_{0,\ldots,v^{\prime}-1}\text{rev}({T_{u,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v}})R_{u+1,\ldots,|R|-1}
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
SP_{a,\ldots,f}
which is a contradiction of the definition of shield
S
.

Finally, if
\mathrm{pos}(T_{v})-\overrightarrow{v}=\mathrm{pos}(P_{v^{\prime}})
for some
a\leq v^{\prime}\leq b_{g}
, then
T_{v}-\overrightarrow{v}=P_{v^{\prime}}
by Lemma
36
. Nevertheless, the existence of
T_{u,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v}
is a contradiction by Corollary
3
.
∎

Of course there exists a version of this lemma obtained by switching the role of the two paths.

Lemma 39
.

Consider the notations
15
, a path
B
such that
e_{B}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and such that
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}-1}B
is producible. If
Q_{p-1}(B+\overrightarrow{v})
is more right-priority (resp. left-priority) than
Q_{p}R^{\prime}
then
B+\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g^{\prime}}}
and does not intersect with
S^{\prime}
and
Q_{a^{\prime},\ldots,p-1}
.

Lemma
38
and Lemma
39
are the patches required to follow the steps of Subsection
7.3
. Indeed, now we can try to grow
R^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
from
P_{0,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
without fearing conflict from
S
or
P_{a,\ldots,b_{g}-1}
. We can also try to grow
R+\overrightarrow{v}
from
Q_{0,\ldots,p-1}
without fearing conflict from
S^{\prime}
or
Q_{a^{\prime},\ldots,p-1}
. These results are the first half of the final Lemma
45
of this section.

7.4.2
Case where
w\neq w^{\prime}

First of all, to simplify the notation we suppose that
w<w^{\prime}
and that
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{g}-1}P_{b_{g}})
is a southern glue (all other cases are symmetric to this one), see Figure
72
. Now, the result here is harder to prove than the one in the case
w=w^{\prime}
but it is also stronger. We will conclude with Lemma
44
which allows to copy a path assembled at the end of
Q_{0,\ldots,p-1}
and translates it by
-\overrightarrow{v}
directly into
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
. To prove this result, we first consider the path
SP_{a,\ldots,b_{g}}
and show that if a path
B
turns right of this path then it is inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
and cannot leave this area.

.

. Consider the path

.

Then

is inside

and does not intersect with

apart from

.

. Suppose that

. Since

, the assembly

. The lemma is true in this case.

Now, to achieve our goal, we just need to show that the hypothesis of Lemma
35
are met for some
Q_{u}-\overrightarrow{v}
with
0\leq u<p
. To do so we will focus at the end of
L
and
L^{\prime}
, especially at the indices
0\leq i\leq|L|-1
and
0\leq i^{\prime}\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
such that

and

respectively.

Lemma 41
.

Consider the notations
15
, there exist
0\leq i\leq|L|
such that
L_{i,\ldots,|L|-1}
is a positive upward arc of glue column
c
and we have
y_{L_{|L|-1}}>y_{L_{0}}\geq y_{L_{i}}\geq y_{P_{m_{g}}}
.

Proof.

With the chosen conventions,
P_{m_{g},\ldots,b_{g}}
is a positive upward arc. By definition of
L
, its last glue is
\mathrm{glue}(P_{b_{g}-1}P_{b_{g}})
and this glue points west on glue column
c
. Since the first glue of
L
is
\mathrm{glue}(P_{h}P_{h+1})
, it is also on glue column
c
. Then, we can define
L_{i,\ldots,|L|-1}
such that this path is an arc of glue column
c
, see Figure
72
. This arc is positive since its last glue points west. Now, the interior of the hole
H=P_{h,\ldots,b_{g}}
corresponds to the left side of
P_{h,\ldots,b_{g}}
. Thus, if there exists
i<j<|L|-1
and
m_{g}<j^{\prime}<b_{g}
such that
\text{rev}({P_{m_{g},\ldots,j^{\prime}}L_{j+1}})
turns left of
\text{rev}({P_{m_{g},\ldots,j^{\prime}+1}})
then the path
L
is not inside the interior of the hole
H
. This is a contradiction, since
L
is defined as
\min(\mathcal{I}_{H})
. Then,
L_{i,\ldots,|L|-1}
is inside the interior of arc
P_{m_{g},\ldots,b_{g}}
. Of course,
L_{i,\ldots,|L|-1}
cannot cross the frontier of
L
apart from the extremities. Thus we have
y_{L_{|L|-1}}>y_{L_{0}}\geq y_{L_{i}}\geq y_{P_{m_{g}}}
and
L_{i,\ldots,|L|-1}
is an upward arc.
∎

The next step is to compare the two holes
L
and
L^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
.

Lemma 42
.

Consider the notations
15
, consider
0\leq u\leq|L|
such that
L_{1,\ldots,u}
is the largest common prefix between
L_{1,\ldots,|L|-1}
and
L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
then:

•

L_{0}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,u+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns right of
L
;

•

L^{\prime}_{u+1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
and
L_{u+1,\ldots,|L|-1}
do not intersect;

•

L_{1,\ldots,|L|-2}
is inside the interior of
L^{\prime}-\overrightarrow{v}
.

Proof.

Since the tile assembly system is directed and since
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,u}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible, we have two cases to study: either
L_{0}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,u+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns left of
L
or
L_{0}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,u+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns right of
L
.

If
L_{0}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,u+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns left of
L
, see Figure
74
. Consider
u\leq v\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
such that
(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v})
is the largest prefix of
(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v})
to be inside
\mathcal{I}_{L}
. Then,
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible. If
L^{\prime}_{u+1,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v}
intersects with
L_{u+1,\ldots,|L|-1}
, then it is possible to contradict the fact
L
is a minimum hole. If
L^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,v}-\overrightarrow{v}
crosses the frontier of the hole
L
, since
w<w^{\prime}
then
L^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,v}
crosses the frontier of the hole
L^{\prime}
contradicting the definition of a hole.
Then
v=|L^{\prime}|-1
. Nevertheless, by Lemma
41
there exists
0\leq i^{\prime}\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
such that
L^{\prime}_{i^{\prime},\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
is a positive upward arc of glue column
c
with
y_{L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}}>y_{L^{\prime}_{0}}\geq y_{L^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}}
. Since this arc is inside the interior of the hole
L
and thus in the interior of the hole
P_{h,\ldots,b_{g}}

then by Lemma
13
, there exists
0\leq i^{\prime\prime}\leq j^{\prime\prime}\leq|L|-1
such that
P_{i^{\prime\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime\prime}}
is a positive arc of glue column
c
such that
\min\{y_{P_{i^{\prime\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\}\leq y_{L^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}}\leq y_{L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}}\leq\max\{y_{P_{i^{\prime\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\}
. Since
\mathrm{pos}(L^{\prime}_{0}-\overrightarrow{v})=\mathrm{pos}(P_{h})
and
w<w^{\prime}
, then
\min\{y_{P_{i^{\prime\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\}\leq y_{P_{h}}<y_{P_{b_{g}}}<y_{L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}}\leq\max\{y_{P_{i^{\prime\prime}}},y_{P_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\}
.
Then, the arc
P_{i^{\prime\prime},\ldots,j^{\prime\prime}}
either crosses or dominates the arc
P_{m_{g},\ldots,b_{g}}
contradicting the fact that
P
is simple or the definition of a main glue.

Lemma 43
.

Consider the notations

. Consider the path

and

) are possible and the lemma is true in this case:
L^{\prime}
enters inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
(the dark blue area). The red arrows (Case
3(b)
) is not possible: the beginning of
F+\overrightarrow{v}
can grow inside the interior of
L^{\prime}
and we are in the previous case of Figure
75
. The yellow arrow (Case
2(b)
) corresponds to the case where
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime},\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
enters inside a blue area. This area is delimited by
F_{b,\ldots,|F|-1}
and
L_{b^{\prime},\ldots,|L|-1}
. In this case
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime},\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
must leave this area by crossing trough
F_{b,\ldots,|F|-1}
and the lemma is true. The fourth case cannot be represented here but is similar to case
3(b)
.

The second case is when
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v}=F_{b+1}
. In this case, let
b^{\prime}<v^{\prime}\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
such that
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime},\ldots,v^{\prime}}
is the largest common prefix between
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime},\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}
and
F_{b,\ldots,|F|-1}
. Let
0\leq v\leq b
such that
F_{v}=L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}
. If
F_{0,\ldots,v}(L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns right of
F
then the lemma is true. Otherwise, by Lemma
42

F_{0,\ldots,v}(L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
cannot turn left of
L
. Then
L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v}
is inside an area delimited by
F_{b,\ldots,|F|-1}
(which is a subpath of
P_{a,\ldots,b}
) and
L_{b^{\prime},\ldots,|L|-1}
, see Figure
76
. to leave this area,
L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}+1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
must cross trough
F_{b,\ldots,|F|-1}
and the Lemma is true.

The third case is when
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}+1}=F_{b-1}
. In this case let
b^{\prime}<v^{\prime}\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
such that
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime},\ldots,v^{\prime}}
is the largest common prefix between
L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime},\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}
and
\text{rev}({F_{b,\ldots,0}})
. Let
0\leq v\leq b
such that
F_{v}=L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}
. If
F_{0,\ldots,v}(L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns right of
F
then the lemma is true. Otherwise,
L^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,v^{\prime}}(F_{v-1}+\overrightarrow{v})
turns left of
L^{\prime}
. Then, as in the proof of Lemma
42
, it is possible to grow
\text{rev}({F_{v-1,\ldots,0}})+\overrightarrow{v}
from
Q_{0,\ldots,h^{\prime}}L^{\prime}_{0,\ldots,v^{\prime}}
inside the interior of
L^{\prime}
. Since
F_{0}+\overrightarrow{v}
is on column
\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})+0.5
then
\text{rev}({F_{v-1,\ldots,0}})+\overrightarrow{v}
will intersect with
L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}+1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}
which contradicts the minimality of the hole
L^{\prime}
.

The fourth case is when
F_{0,\ldots,b}(L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns left of
F
. We remind that
L_{0,\ldots,b}(L^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}+1}-\overrightarrow{v})
turns right of
L
by Lemma
42
. In this case, we can contradict the minimality of the hole
L^{\prime}
as in the previous paragraph.

∎

We can combine Lemma
40
and Lemma
43
to obtain the desired result.

Lemma 44
.

Consider the notations
15
, the path
F=SP_{a,\ldots,b_{g}}
and a path
B
with
e_{B}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c^{\prime})
and such that
Q_{0,\ldots,p-1}B
is producible. There exists
0\leq v^{\prime}<p
and
0<v<|F|-1
such that
(Q_{v^{\prime},\ldots,p-1}-\overrightarrow{v})(B-\overrightarrow{v})
is a subassembly of
\gamma
and
Q_{v^{\prime}}=F_{v}
. Moreover, the path
(B-\overrightarrow{v})
is inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
and does not intersect with
FR_{1,\ldots,|R|-1}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
40
and Lemma
43
, we can deduce that there exist
0\leq v^{\prime\prime}\leq|L^{\prime}|-1
and
0\leq v\leq|F|-1
such that
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible and
F_{0,\ldots,v}L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime\prime}+1}
turns right of
F
, see Figure
78
. Then, all intersections between
L^{\prime}
and
F
are agreements. From
L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}=Q_{p}-\overrightarrow{v}
, we can try to assemble
\text{rev}({Q_{0,\ldots,p}})-\overrightarrow{v}
until it intersect with
F
.

Since
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p-1}Q_{p})
is a “backup” protected glue, there exists
0\leq m<p
such that
Q_{m,\ldots,p}
is a positive dominant upward arc of
Q
on glue column
c^{\prime}
. By Lemma
40
,
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p-1}Q_{p})-\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{R}_{g}
and on glue column
c
and we have
y_{Q_{p}-\overrightarrow{v}}>y_{P_{b_{g}}}
. By definition of
\overrightarrow{v}
, we have
y_{Q_{m}-\overrightarrow{v}}\leq y_{Q_{h^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}}<y_{P_{b_{g}}}
. We also have
w_{Q_{m,\ldots,p}}<\mathcal{B}_{4}(c)
and then the intersection between
Q_{m,\ldots,p}-\overrightarrow{v}
and
F
exists. Let
m\leq v^{\prime}\leq p
such that
Q_{v^{\prime},\ldots,p}
is the shortest suffix of
Q_{m,\ldots,p}
to intersect with
F
.

If
Q_{v^{\prime}}\neq F_{v}
then we can extract form
\mathrm{asm}{(Q_{v^{\prime},\ldots,p})}\cup\mathrm{asm}{(F_{v,\ldots,|F|-1})}
a path which contradicts Lemma
40
. Then,
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime\prime}-1}-\overrightarrow{v})(Q_{v,\ldots,p}-\overrightarrow{v})
is producible. If a path
B
is such that
Q_{0,\ldots,p-1}B
is producible then all intersections between
B
and
L^{\prime}
are agreements. Then by Lemma
40
,
P_{0,\ldots,h}(L^{\prime}_{1,\ldots,v^{\prime\prime}-1}-\overrightarrow{v})(Q_{v,\ldots,p-1}-\overrightarrow{v})(B-\overrightarrow{v})
is a producible path,
B-\overrightarrow{v}
is inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
and does not intersect with
FR_{1,\ldots,|R|-1}
.

P_{h}
L_{i}
P_{m_{g}}
P_{b_{g}}
L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}=Q_{p}-\overrightarrow{v}
P_{f}=R_{|R|-2}

Proof of Lemma
44
: In Figure
76
, we have studied the possible trajectories of
L^{\prime}_{u,\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
(the yellow diamonds). We represent here Case
3(a)
, where this path follows
\text{rev}({F_{0,\ldots,b}})
until
L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}=F_{v}
. Then
L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime\prime},\ldots,|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}
can be assembled inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
without intersecting

by Lemma

. The path
\text{rev}({Q_{0,\ldots,p}-\overrightarrow{v}})
(the orange diamonds) can be assembled from
L^{\prime}_{|L^{\prime}|-1}-\overrightarrow{v}=Q_{p}-\overrightarrow{v}
until it intersects with

at
Q_{v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}
. We must have
F_{v}=Q_{v^{\prime}}
otherwise by following the red arrow from
L^{\prime}_{v^{\prime\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}
to
Q_{v^{\prime}}-\overrightarrow{v}
, we obtain a path which contradicts Lemma

. Now for any path
B
such that
Q_{0,\ldots,p-1}B
is producible,
B-\overrightarrow{v}
can be assembled inside
\mathcal{R}_{b_{g}}
without intersecting
FR_{1,\ldots,|R|-1}
by Lemma
40
.

∎

Now, we have the second half of the final Lemma
45
of this section.

7.4.3
Conclusion for combining two protected “backup” glues

Now, that we have dealt with the two cases, we can conclude this section and solve Lock
4
.

Lemma 45
.

Consider the notations
15
. If there exists an index
p^{\prime}\geq p
such that the
\mathrm{glue}(Q_{p^{\prime}}Q_{p^{\prime}+1})
is a protected glue (either a “main” or “backup” one) of the northern (resp. southern) decomposition of
Q
into dominant arcs then either there exists
g<i<t
such that
b_{i}
is protected or there exists
g<i\leq t
such that
m_{i}
is protected.

Proof.

Using Lemma
38
and Lemma
39
if
w=w^{\prime}
or Lemma
44
if
w\neq w^{\prime}
, it is possible to follow the same steps as in Subsection
7.3
to find a new protected tile with the required properties.
∎

7.5
Conclusion

Theorem 2
.

Consider a directed tile assembly system
(T,\sigma,1)
, if its terminal assembly
\gamma
is finite then its horizontal width and vertical height are bounded by
7|\sigma|+58|T|+30
.

Proof.

For the sake of contradiction, suppose that
e_{\gamma}>\mathcal{B}_{5}
. For each glue column
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
, we consider a canonical path
P^{(c)}
. Note that there are
4|T|+1
of these paths. For each path
P^{(c)}
, we consider the index of the last protected glue of its northern decomposition
{n_{c}}
and the index of the last protected glue of its southern decomposition
s_{c}
. The path
P^{(c)}
is said to be be protected from the south if
s_{c}\leq{n_{c}}
and protected from the north if
s_{c}\geq{n_{c}}
. In the special case where
s_{c}={n_{c}}
, we consider that this path is protected from the north and from the south.

Among these paths, either at least
2|T|+1
of them are protected from the north or at least
2|T|+1
of them are protected from the south. Without loss of generality, we consider the later case. Consider the category of the last protected glue of the southern decomposition of these paths: at least
|T|+1
of these glues are in the category “main” or at least
|T|+1
of these glues are in the category “back-up”. Then, we can conclude that there exist
\mathcal{B}_{1}\leq c<c^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{B}_{2}
such that
P^{(c)}
and
P^{(c^{\prime})}
are protected from the south, and their last protected glue of their southern decomposition have the same category and type. Thus, by Lemma
34
(if the category of these two glues is “main”) or
45
(if the category of these two glues is “back-up”), we obtain the contradiction that
s_{c}
is not the last protected glue of the southern decomposition of
P^{(c)}
.

The value
w_{\gamma}
can be bounded similarly and then the horizontal width of
\gamma
can be bounded by
7|\sigma|+58|T|+30
. The same reasoning can be done for the vertical height.
∎
